Black Flowers and Real Forms of Higher Spin Symmetries
R. Sammani, E.H Saidi

Abstract
Using Chern-Simons formulation, we investigate higher spin (HS) black holes in AdS 3 with soft Heisenberg hair and establish linkage with the real forms of the underlying complexified gauge symmetries taken here as S ​ L ​ ( N , ℂ ) 𝙻 × S ​ L ​ ( N , ℂ ) 𝚁 . SL(N,\mathbb{C})_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(N,\mathbb{C})_{\mathtt{R}}. We study the various conserved currents characterizing the HS black flowers (HS-BF) and show that they can be formed of layers indexed by the elements of the centre of the gauge symmetry. This feature follows from requiring the holonomy of the asymptotic gauge connection around the thermal cycle to sit in the centre ℤ N \mathbb{Z}_{N} of the symmetry group. With regard to the compact subgroups of the real forms of the complexified gauge symmetry, we calculate the entropies of the HS-BF and verify that, unless we are considering trivial holonomies, there are no continuous paths joining the HS-BF to the core spin 2 black holes. As explicit illustrations, we give quantum field realisations of the soft Heisenberg hair in terms of bosonic and fermionic primary conformal fields and compute the HS-BF entropy as a function of the number of fermions occupying the ground state of the Heisenberg soft hair.

Introduction

Since their inauguration in
[
1
]
, the three
dimensional black hole solutions of AdS
3
gravity in the Chern-Simons
formulation
[
2
,
3
]
were built in accordance with the well established
Brown-Henneaux boundary conditions (BC)
[
4
]
. However, over the few
past years, it was evidenced that higher spin AdS
3
gravity admits
stationary black hole solutions that transcend the customary Brown-Henneaux
BC
[
5
,
6
]
. An example of such configuration is the spin
s=2
gravity
model constructed in
[
5
]
; it features (non) spherical black holes with

SL(2,\mathbb{R})_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(2,\mathbb{R})_{\mathtt{R}}
gauge
symmetry yielding two copies of the asymptotic affine û(1)
k

Heisenberg algebras generated by an infinite number of conserved charges (
J_{m}
,
\bar{J}_{\bar{m}}
) labelled by the integers
m
and
\bar{m}
. The
often anticipated Brown-Henneaux boundary conformal symmetry emerges as a
composite invariance through a twisted Sugawara construction and the
regularity of the gauge configurations at the black hole (BH) horizon was
confirmed to hold independently of the global charges. In addition, these
black holes carry soft hair states
[
9
,
10
]
with BH entropy S
{}_{\text{{bh}}}
given by the remarkable relation

S_{\text{{bh}}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathbf{n}\left(J_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}\right)

(1)

where
\mathbf{n}
is a positive integer and where the
J_{0}
and
\bar{J}_{0}
are the zero modes commuting with all of the excited
J_{m},

\bar{J}_{\bar{m}}
modes generating the affine
\hat{U}(1)_{\mathrm{k}}\times\hat{U}(1)_{\mathrm{\bar{k}}}
algebra.

Higher spin (HS) extensions
[
11
]
-
[
14
]
of the previous spin s=2
AdS
3
gravity model were developed in
[
6
]
for the larger
SL(N,\mathbb{R})_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(N,\mathbb{R})_{\mathtt{R}}
gauge symmetry
with an emphasis on the N=3 case for illustrations. The associated
asymptotic algebra is given by a greater product of affine Heisenberg
symmetries
\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}_{i}\hat{U}(1)_{\mathrm{k}_{i}}\times\hat{U}(1)_{\mathrm{\bar{k}}_{i}}

[
15
]
; and similarly to the spin s=2 theory, the boundary
W
N
-invariance
[
17
]
-
[
19
]
can be built out of composites of
Kac-Moody (KM) charges via an extended twisted Sugawara
[
20
]
. The
generalised HS theory comprises black hole solutions dubbed higher spin
black flowers (HS-BF
{}_{\text{{n}}}
)
[
16
]
whose entropy S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}
for the
SL(3)_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(3)_{\mathtt{R}}
model has the form

S_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathbf{n}\left(J_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}\right)+3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathbf{m}\left(K_{0}+\bar{K}_{0}\right)

(2)

where GPPTT credits the authors Grumiller- Perez- Prohazka- Tempo- Troncoso.
The parameters
\mathbf{n}
and
\mathbf{m}
are positive integers, and the
zero mode charges (
J_{0},K_{0};\bar{J}_{0},\bar{K}_{0}
) belong to the
tensorial affine Heisenberg
\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}_{i=1}^{2}\hat{U}(1
)
{}_{\mathrm{k}_{i}}\times\hat{U}(1)_{\mathrm{\bar{k}}_{i}}
algebra generated by the
infinite sets (
J_{n},K_{m}
) and (
\bar{J}_{\bar{n}},\bar{K}_{\bar{m}}
).
By defining
\mathtt{I}_{0}^{{\small L}}=\mathbf{n}J_{0}+3\mathbf{m}K_{0}/2

for the left sector and
\mathtt{I}_{0}^{{\small R}}=\mathbf{n}\bar{J}_{0}+3\mathbf{m}\bar{K}_{0}/2
for the right one, the above entropy formula can be
concisely presented as
2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}
(
\mathtt{I}_{0}^{{\small L}}+\mathtt{I}_{0}^{{\small R}}).
Moreover, we are able to recuperate the fundamental HS black
hole entropy formula of
[
21
]
from the S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}
entropy of the higher black flower, as it is continuously connected to the spin 2 BTZ black hole
[
6
]
. By setting
\mathbf{m}=0
and
\mathbf{n}=1
in the above spin 3 formula, one
recovers the entropy
2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(J_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}\right)
of the spin s=2
BH with gauge symmetry
SL(2)_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(2)_{\mathtt{R}}
and
boundary conditions as in
[
5
]
.

In this paper, we develop the study of higher spin black flowers (HS-BF
{}_{\text{{n}}}
) entropy with
SL(N,\mathbb{R})_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(N,\mathbb{R})_{\mathtt{R}}
gauge symmetry and generalised boundary conditions
as in eqs(
5
-
6
). We consider non trivial holonomies around
thermal cycles taking values in the centre
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
of the gauge
invariance. This discrete group is given by the set
\left\langle{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}\right\rangle_{1\leq p<N}
with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}=\exp(2i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}/N),
it permits
to extend the trivial holonomies of
[
5
,
6
,
21
]
beyond the identity
element
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{0}=I_{id}
corresponding to
p=0.
With this enlarged set
of holonomies, we show amongst others that higher spin black flowers possess
N layers labelled by the central group elements
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}
with
1\leq p<N
. And for each layer of the HS-BF
{}_{\text{{n}}}
, the entropy S
{}_{\text{{hs-bf}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{N}}
is a function of the
rational number p/N (mod N) such that

2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left|\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{l}}}(p/N)\right|+2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left|\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{r}}}(p/N)\right|

(3)

where
\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{l}}}(p/N)
and
\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{r}}}(p/N)
are HS gauge invariants given by linear combinations of
the commuting conserved charges like
\sum_{s=2}^{N}\Lambda_{p,\mathbf{n}_{s}}^{{\small(s-1)}}J_{0}^{{\small(s)}}
. In this latter expansion, the
J_{0}^{{\small(s)}}
s are the zero modes of the affine Heisenberg-like
symmetry
\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\hat{U}(1)_{\mathrm{k}_{i}}\times\hat{U}(1)_{\mathrm{\bar{k}}_{i}}
while the
\Lambda_{p,\mathbf{n}_{s}}^{{\small(s-1)}}
’s
designate quantized Lagrange multipliers determined by solving the
regularity condition of the holonomy of the asymptotic gauge potentials.
Contrary to what was obtained in
[
6
]
for the N=3 theory with trivial
holonomy, there are no continuous paths joining the black flowers entropy S
{}_{\text{{hs-bf}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{3}}
to the core S
{}_{\text{{hs-bf}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{2}}
, and more generally no continuous
bridges leading any S
{}_{\text{{hs-bf}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{N}}

towards S
{}_{\text{{hs-bf}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{M}}
with
M<N,

once we consider holonomy layers beyond the identity
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{0}=I_{id}.

Therefore, descending from a higher black flower to a lower BF requires
discrete leaps given by Weyl group transformations of the HS gauge algebra.

Besides the disrupted continuity, we uncover additional features. To
rigorously study the thermal properties of higher spin black flowers
including their entropies, one must promote the real gauge symmetry
SL(N,\mathbb{R})_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(N,\mathbb{R})_{\mathtt{R}}
to one of the real forms of the complexified
SL(N,\mathbb{C})_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(N,\mathbb{C})_{\mathtt{R}};
a property previously noted for the Euclidean HS
gravity investigated in
[
21
]
(see table I therein). As a
result, new aspects of HS-BF
{}_{\text{{n}}}
emerge; in particular
the close-knit relation between the branches of the HS black flowers and the
real forms of the underlying complexified gauge symmetry which is showcased
by the dependency of the entropy on the type of the real form.

In sum, and before delving into the specifics, we list here below some of
our main contributions to the study of HS AdS
3
gravities having HS
black flowers solutions with
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
generalised
boundary gauge constraints beyond the standard Brown-Henneaux conditions and

(\mathbf{ii})
asymptotic gauge potential holonomies sitting in the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
centre:

•

We first describe the extended BC of
[
5
,
6
]
and exploit
this construction to develop a new HS basis

using the roots of the
Lie algebra of the gauge symmetry with illustrative examples. It is founded
on the Cartan-Weyl basis and is equivalent to the more usual one using the
principal
SL_{2}
representations.

In this new basis, the left (
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},a_{t}
) and the right (
\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\tilde{a}_{t}
)
diagonal boundary gauge potentials of
[
6
]
have vanishing commutators
[a_{t},a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}]
and
[\tilde{a}_{t},\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}]
as they are
taken in the Cartan subalgebra of the HS gauge invariance. Accordingly, the
associated gauge field strengths reduce to flat Maxwell-like gauge
curvatures given by
\partial_{t}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}-\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}a_{t}=0.

We then consider the
a_{t}
components as Lagrange multipliers constrained
like
\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}a_{t}=0
(say
a_{t}=
constant), giving in result

\partial_{t}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}=0
and
\partial_{t}\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}=0
.
These boundary potential constraints are to be interpreted as boundary
fields equations following from a 2D variational principle with Lagrange
field parameters. As a result, our approach leads directly to the conserved
total Hamiltonian and the conserved angular momentum of the HS theory with
an infinite asymptotic conserved
\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}_{i}\hat{U}(1)_{\mathrm{k}_{i}}\times\hat{U}(1)_{\mathrm{\bar{k}}_{i}}
charges while allowing us to
retrieve known results in HS literature.

•

Our construction also enables to interconnect the computation of the
BH entropy, requiring regularity of the asymptotic gauge configurations
around the thermal cycle, with the compact subgroups of the real forms of
the complexified gauge symmetry. In fact in order to derive the thermal BH
properties (
129
-
130
), one needs to perform a Wick rotation which
leads to a complexification of the
SL(N,\mathbb{R})
gauge symmetry towards
one of the real forms of
SL(N,\mathbb{C})
. In this regards, we give a
concrete description of the HS branches observed in
[
6
]
using real
forms of
SL(N,\mathbb{C})_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(N,\mathbb{C})_{\mathtt{R}}

including the real splits
SL(N,\mathbb{R})
, the compact
SU(N)
and the
non compact
SU(N_{1},N_{2})
s. Moreover by allowing non trivial gauge
holonomies of the boundary potentials, that is holonomies sitting in the
centre
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
of the complexified gauge symmetry
SL(N,\mathbb{C})
, we find that the HS black flowers have several dissociated layers that
cannot be continuously connected to the spin 2 AdS
3
black holes. The
crossing between the various layers of the HS-BF
{}_{\text{{n}}}

requires discrete transitions as opposed to the prevailing consensus on HS-BF
{}_{\text{{n}}}
with trivial holonomies.

•

Exploiting the conformal symmetry at the boundary of the HS-AdS
3

gravity, we give bosonic and fermionic oscillator realisations of the BF
particle excitations. We first recall properties on the free fields of
compact and non compact affine KM-symmetries which are known to be closely
related to the sign of the CS level k as first noticed by Dixon,
Peskin, and Lykken for the SU(2)
k
and SL(2,
\mathbb{R}
)
k
conformal models; and successfully used in several applications
like in recent studies regarding string theory on
AdS_{3}\times S^{3}\times M
with one unit of NS-NS flux (k = 1)
[
22
,
23
]
and refs
therein. For example, the operator product of the current operator
J_{{\small su}_{{\small 2}}}^{0}\left(z\right)
of the compact SU(2)
k
displays a positive CS level
+\mathrm{k}
as shown by
J_{{\small su}_{{\small 2}}}^{0}\left(z\right)J_{{\small su}_{{\small 2}}}^{0}\left(w\right)\sim+\mathrm{k}/(z-w)^{2}
; unlike its homologue for
the non compact SL(2,
\mathbb{R}
)
k
, it exhibits a negative CS
level
\mathrm{\tilde{k}=}-\mathrm{k}
as shown by the corresponding OPE
namely
J_{{\small sl}_{{\small 2},\mathbb{R}}}^{0}\left(z\right)J_{{\small sl}_{{\small 2},\mathbb{R}}}^{0}\left(w\right)\sim-\mathrm{k}/(z-w)^{2}.
Focusing on the Cartan sectors of the real forms of SL(N,
\mathbb{C}
)
k
with emphasis on the compact SU(N,
\mathbb{C}
)
k
, we give the quantum numbers characterising the A
N-1
soft hair BH, describe the ground state of this black flowers and
comment on their critical properties. For the bosonic realisation of the
spin s=3 black flower, we show that the zero modes of the primary scalar
fields sit in the
\mathbb{Z}^{4}
lattice as given by eqs(
155
). As for the fermionic oscillator realisation, we distinguish two pictures
given by periodic and anti-periodic boundary fermions. For both of these
(anti-) periodicities, we find amongst others that the entropy of the black
flowers (
179
) can be interpreted in terms of the number of fermionic
particles living in the ground state of the HS-BF soft hair.

The organisation of this paper is as follows: In
section 2
, we
describe the generalised boundary conditions of
[
5
,
6
]
and

set
up the basic tools of the CS formulation in preparation for the upcoming
study of black holes solutions. In
section 3
, we give new
results on higher spin black flowers physics. In particular, we establish an
affiliation between the thermodynamics of black hole solutions and the real
forms of the complexified gauge symmetry of the HS AdS
3
gravity. We

utilise

field theory techniques on constrained hamiltonian
to develop a method that allows to build the boundary field action. All for
the purpose of deriving the HS black flowers entropies for the various real
forms of the complex gauge group. In
section 4
, we derive

the affine Heisenberg soft hair algebra with regard to the considered
real forms and explicitly compute the entropy formulas for the various black
flowers branches. In
section 5
, we realise the charge
potentials in terms of bosonic and fermionic primary conformal fields on the
horizon of the black hole flowers as concrete illustrations of our analysis.
Last
section 6
is devoted to conclusion and discussions. We
accompany our work with three appendices A, B and C. In
Appendix A
, we build HS AdS
3
theories using real forms of complex Lie
algebras with the help of Tits-Satake diagrams. In
Appendix B
, we compute the asymptotic current algebra of the boundary invariants
operators. And finally in
Appendix C
, we study the physical
significance of the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
centre symmetry behind the non trivial
holonomies.

2
Asymptotic potentials in AdS
3

In this section, we lay the groundwork for our study by
describing useful tools on higher spin (HS) AdS
3
gravity formulated in
terms of two copies of Chern-Simons potentials A
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\left(r,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t\right)
and Ã
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\left(r,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t\right)
with opposite CS couplings (
\mathrm{\tilde{k}}=-\mathrm{k}
). These bulk potentials are
valued in the Lie algebra of the HS gauge symmetry with some boundary
conditions

A_{r}

\rightarrow

A_{r}^{{\small asym}}

\simeq

g^{-1}\partial_{r}g

A_{\pm}

\rightarrow

A_{\pm}^{{\small asym}}

\simeq

g^{-1}a_{\pm}g+g^{-1}\partial_{\pm}g

(4)

characterised by the gauge transformation
g
taken as in
[
5
,
6
]
. The
asymptotic

and

sit in the near horizon of the HS black holes (BH); they are generated by
the boundary gauge potentials

investigated hereafter.

2.1
Structure of boundary potentials

We start by introducing the structure of the connections
a
and
\tilde{a}

living in the 2D boundary surface
\Sigma_{{\small 2D}}=\partial\mathcal{M}_{{\small 3D}}
of the higher spin AdS
3
gravity expressed in terms of
two Chern-Simons potentials. These boundary fields
a\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t\right)
and
\tilde{a}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t\right)
are local 1-forms
valued in the Lie algebra of the gauge symmetry
G\times\tilde{G}.
They
allow to build the three dimensional CS gauge potentials
A
and
\tilde{A}

(
4
) of the bulk
\mathcal{M}_{{\small 3D}}
through the following boundary condition

A=\mathfrak{b}^{-1}\left(d+a\right)\mathfrak{b}\qquad,\qquad\tilde{A}=\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}^{-1}\left(d+\tilde{a}\right)\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}

(5)

The expressions of
\mathfrak{b}
and
\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}
are given by
e^{-L_{+}/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}_{-}}e^{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}L_{-}/2}
and
e^{+L_{+}/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}_{-}}e^{+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}L_{-}/2}

[

,

g=e^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{-}L_{+}}e^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{0}L_{0}}e^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{+}L_{-}}
with real gauge
parameters
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{0,\pm}
and the pure gauge 1-form

expanding

g^{-1}dg=L_{-}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{+}+\left(L_{0}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{+}L_{-}\right)d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{0}+\left(L_{+}+2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{+}L_{0}+2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{+}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{+}L_{-}\right)d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{-}

(6)

where the
L_{0,\pm}
generate the principal SL(2,
\mathbb{R}
).
Interesting examples of such (non) spherical boundary conditions include the
three following:
(\mathbf{i})
the
\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{+},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{0},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{-}\right)\sim\left(0,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}},0\right)
used in
[

,
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

Recall that the 3D field action of this HS gauge theory is given by the

\mathcal{S}_{{\small 3D}}=\frac{\mathrm{k}}{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\int tr(AdA\mathbf{+}\frac{2}{3}A^{3}\mathbf{)}-\frac{\mathrm{k}}{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\int tr(\tilde{A}d\tilde{A}\mathbf{+}\frac{2}{3}\tilde{A}^{3}\mathbf{)}

2.2

2.2.1
Highest weight representation

We give the HWR for the simplest higher spin models

with small

G
gauge symmetries
.
This is the case of
\left(\mathbf{a}\right)
the basic spin s=2 gravity with symmetry

having a rank

and
\left(\mathbf{b}\right)
the two spins gravities with rank
\mathtt{r}_{G}=2.

For instance, we cite the familiar HS model with
SL\left(3,\mathbb{R}\right)_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL\left(3,\mathbb{R}\right)_{\mathtt{R}}

algebra having the spins {
s_{1}=2,s_{2}=3

For the basic spin s=2 model, the HWR of the boundary gauge connection in

follows

a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{hw}}}

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{+}
