a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{+}=1
. The set {
W_{m_{s}}^{(s)}
}
designate the
\ N^{2}-1
generators of
SL\left(N,\mathbb{R}\right)

labelled by two integers
\left(s,m_{s}\right)
taking values as
2\leq s\leq N
and
1-s\leq m_{s}\leq s-1.
Similar relations can be written as
well for the potentials
\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
and
\tilde{a}_{t}
of the
right sector.

Before proceeding, take note of the

following:

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)

The three leading
W_{m_{2}}^{(s=2)}
are
just the three generators
L_{0,\pm}
of the principal
SL\left(2,\mathbb{R}\right)
satisfying the commutation relations
[L_{n},L_{m}]=\left(n-m\right)L_{n+m}
. In the isospin
j=1/2
representation, we have the 2
\times
2 matrix realisation

L_{+}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 1}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right),\quad L_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{{\small 1}}{2}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small-}\frac{{\small 1}}{2}\end{array}\right),\quad L_{-}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\small 0}&-{\small 1}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right)

(20)

acting on the two eigenvectors
L_{0}\left|q\right\rangle=q\left|q\right\rangle
with
q=\pm 1/2.

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)

The angular components {
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small(1-s)}},\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small(s-1)}}
} termed as the
charge potentials are commonly scaled by a (2
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}/\mathrm{k}
) factor. For the spin s=2, they are often denoted like
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{-}:=a_{++}
and
\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{+}:=\tilde{a}_{--}
with

a_{++}=-\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{\mathrm{k}}\mathcal{L}_{++}\qquad,\qquad\tilde{a}_{--}=-\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{\mathrm{k}}\mathcal{\tilde{L}}_{--}

(21)

More generally, we have
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small(1-s)}}=-\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{s}}{\mathrm{k}}\mathcal{W}_{+s}
and
\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small(s-1)}}=-\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{s}}{\mathrm{k}}\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{-s}
with some
normalisation numbers
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{s}
required by the canonical commutation
relations of the W-symmetry
[
3
,
19
]
. For the spin s=3 example,
we get
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{--}=-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{2\mathrm{k}}\mathcal{W}_{+3}
and
\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{--}=-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{2\mathrm{k}}\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{-3}.

The charge potentials (
\mathcal{L}_{++},\mathcal{W}_{+s})
and (
\mathcal{\tilde{L}}_{--},\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{-s})
are conserved quantities
(constant of motion), they will be further discussed later on (see
subsection 3.2).

\left(\mathbf{3}\right)

The time components
a_{t}^{{\small(m}_{s}{\small)}}
will fulfill the role of Lagrange multipliers, they are
constrained by the field equations (
12
) and interpreted as chemical
potentials in the thermodynamics of HS- black holes
[
12
,
33
,
34
,
35
]
.

To truly understand the usefulness of the HW representation, we take on
the simple HS model with
SL\left(3,\mathbb{R}\right)
symmetry. This
group has eight traceless generators split in two subsets as
3+5:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the isotriplet
W_{0,\pm}^{(s=2)}=L_{0,\pm}
generate
the principal SL
\left(2,\mathbb{R}\right)
represented as

L_{+}=\sqrt{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 1}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 1}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right),\quad L_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\small 1}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small-1}\end{array}\right),\quad L_{-}=\sqrt{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\small 0}&-{\small 1}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&-{\small 1}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right)

(22)

with real
\left(L_{n}\right)^{\ast}=L_{n}
and
\left(L_{n}\right)^{\dagger}=\left(-\right)^{n}L_{-n}
as well as

Tr\left(L_{0}L_{0}\right)=2,\qquad Tr\left(L_{-}L_{+}\right)=-4

(23)

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
the 5-uplet
W_{0,\pm 1,\pm 2}^{(s=3)}
forming
a 5-dim representation of SL
\left(2,\mathbb{R}\right)
are given by

W_{+2}

=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 4}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right)

,

W_{+}

=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
\sqrt{{\small 2}}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small-}\sqrt{{\small 2}}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right)

W_{0}

=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{2}{3}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small-}\frac{4}{3}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&\frac{2}{3}\end{array}\right)

W_{-2}

=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 4}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right)

,

W_{-}

=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\small 0}&{\small-}\sqrt{{\small 2}}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&\sqrt{{\small 2}}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right)

(24)

where
\left(W_{n}\right)^{\ast}=W_{n}
and
\left(W_{n}\right)^{\dagger}=\left(-\right)^{n}W_{-n}
as well as

Tr\left(W_{0}W_{0}\right)=\frac{8}{3},\qquad Tr\left(W_{-}W_{+}\right)=-4,\qquad Tr\left(W_{-2}W_{+2}\right)=16

(25)

with the remarkable orthogonality relation
Tr\left(L_{0}W_{0}\right)=0.
In this particular higher spin model, the potentials
read as follows:

a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{hw}}}

=

L_{+}+a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{-}L_{-}+a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{--}W_{--}

a_{t}^{\text{{hw}}}

=

\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{m_{2}=-1}^{+1}a_{t}^{m_{2}}L_{m_{2}}+\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{m_{3}=-2}^{+2}a_{t}^{m_{3}}W_{m_{3}}

(26)

with the constraint relations (
12
). As noticed before, this
realisation can be recast like

a_{+}^{\text{{hw}}}

=

L_{+}-\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{\mathrm{k}}\mathcal{L}_{++}L_{-}-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{2\mathrm{k}}\mathcal{W}_{+3}W_{--}

a_{-}^{\text{{hw}}}

=

\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{m_{2}=-1}^{+1}a_{-}^{m_{2}}L_{m_{2}}+\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{m_{3}=-2}^{+2}a_{-}^{m_{3}}W_{m_{3}}

(27)

where the components
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{hw}}}
and
a_{t}^{\text{{hw}}}
can be interpreted as
a_{+}^{\text{{hw}}}
(charges)
and
a_{-}^{\text{{hw}}}
(Lagrange multipliers).

2.2.2
Diagonal representation

For the rest of the analysis, we settle on this type of representation and
set our boundary fields to obey the diagonal form. For the spin 3 case, the
boundary potentials are valued in the Cartan subalgebra of
SL\left(3,\mathbb{R}\right)
generated by the two commuting
L_{0}
and
W_{0}
with
the orthogonal property
Tr(L_{0}W_{0})=0.
The 1-form connections read as
follows

a^{\text{{diag}}}

=

\left(\Upsilon^{0}+\mathcal{J}^{0}\right)L_{0}+\left(\Gamma^{0}+\mathcal{K}^{0}\right)W_{0}

\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}

=

(\tilde{\Upsilon}^{0}-\mathcal{\tilde{J}}^{0})L_{0}+(\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}-\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0})W_{0}

(28)

with
L_{0}
and
W_{0}
as in (
24
). They are real potentials as they
verify:

\displaystyle(a^{\text{{diag}}})^{\ast}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a^{\text{{diag}}}\qquad,\qquad(\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}})^{\ast}=\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}

\displaystyle(a^{\text{{diag}}})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a^{\text{{diag}}}\qquad,\qquad(\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}})^{\dagger}=\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}

(29)

Notice that for the particular case where
\Gamma^{0}=\mathcal{K}^{0}=0

and
\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}=\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}=0
, the boundary
potentials reduce to
a^{\text{{diag}}}=\left(\Upsilon^{0}+\mathcal{J}^{0}\right)L_{0}
and
\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}=(\tilde{\Upsilon}^{0}-\mathcal{\tilde{J}}^{0})L_{0}
meaning that they are in fact
valued in the principal subset
sl\left(2,\mathbb{R}\right)
of
sl\left(3,\mathbb{R}\right)
.

In the above (
28
), the partial connections are given by

\mathcal{J}^{0}

=\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}

,\qquad

\mathcal{K}^{0}

=\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}

\Upsilon^{0}

=\Upsilon_{t}^{0}dt

,\qquad

\Gamma^{0}

=\Gamma_{t}^{0}dt

(30)

and

\mathcal{\tilde{J}}^{0}

=\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}

,\qquad

\mathcal{\tilde{K}}^{0}

=\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}

\tilde{\Upsilon}^{0}

=\tilde{\Upsilon}_{t}^{0}dt

,\qquad

\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}

=\tilde{\Gamma}_{t}^{0}dt

(31)

with the reality property

(\mathcal{J}^{0})^{\ast}=\mathcal{J}^{0},\qquad(\mathcal{K}^{0})^{\ast}=\mathcal{K}^{0},\qquad(\mathcal{\tilde{J}}^{0})^{\ast}=\mathcal{\tilde{J}}^{0},\qquad(\mathcal{\tilde{K}}^{0})^{\ast}=\mathcal{\tilde{K}}^{0}

(32)

The use of partial 1-form potentials is essential as
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
it distinguishes the charges (
\mathcal{J},\mathcal{K};\mathcal{\tilde{J}},\mathcal{\tilde{K}}
) from the
Lagrange multipliers (
\Upsilon,\Gamma;\tilde{\Upsilon},\tilde{\Gamma}
);
and
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
differentiates between the two
diagonal gl(1,
\mathbb{R}
)
J
and gl(1,
\mathbb{R}
)
K
for each copy of sl(3,
\mathbb{R}
). Hence,
the components of
a^{\text{{diag}}}
and
\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}
are defined as

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{diag}}}$&$=$&$\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}L_{0}+\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}W_{0}$\\
$a_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}$&$=$&$\Upsilon_{t}^{0}L_{0}+\Gamma_{t}^{0}W_{0}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{diag}}}$&$=$&$-\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}L_{0}-\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}W_{0}$\\
$\tilde{a}_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}$&$=$&$\tilde{\Upsilon}_{t}^{0}L_{0}+\tilde{\Gamma}_{t}^{0}W_{0}$\end{tabular}

(33)

Note that the diagonal potentials
(a^{\text{{diag}}},\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}})
are related to the highest weight gauge
potentials
(a^{\text{{hw}}},\tilde{a}^{\text{{hw}}})

through
SL\left(3,\mathbb{R}\right)
gauge transformations
a^{\text{{hw}}}=g_{\text{{bridge}}}^{-1}a^{\text{{diag}}}g_{\text{{bridge}}}
and
\tilde{a}^{\text{{hw}}}=\tilde{g}_{\text{{bridge}}}^{-1}\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}\tilde{g}_{\text{{bridge}}}
with commutative diagram

Bulk

:

A^{\text{{hw}}}

\quad-\boldsymbol{G}_{I}-\rightarrow\quad

A^{\text{{diag}}}

\downarrow

asymp
.

\mathbf{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{G}_{II}^{\prime}

\mathbf{\downarrow}\boldsymbol{G}_{II}

Boundary

:

a^{\text{{hw}}}

\quad-\boldsymbol{G}_{I}^{\prime}-\rightarrow\quad

a^{\text{{diag}}}

(34)

where (
\boldsymbol{G}_{I},\boldsymbol{G}_{II}
) and (
\boldsymbol{G}_{I}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{G}_{II}^{\prime}
) are some gauge
transformations. The bridge transformations between
a^{\text{{hw}}}

and
a^{\text{{diag}}}
have the structure
g_{\text{{bridge}}}=g_{1}g_{2}
with
g_{1}=\exp({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 274$}}}_{-}L_{+}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}_{-}W_{+}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}_{-2}W_{+2})
and
g_{2}=\exp(\mathcal{F}_{+}L_{-}+\mathcal{G}_{+}W_{-}+\mathcal{G}_{+2}W_{-2})
. They were computed in
[
6
]
where it was shown
that the left charges (
\mathcal{L}_{++},\mathcal{W}_{+3})\equiv(\mathcal{L}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{W}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}})
and their twild
homologue (
\mathcal{\tilde{L}}_{--},\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{-3})\equiv(\mathcal{\tilde{L}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}})
are related to (
\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0},\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0})
and (
\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0},\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}
) as follows

\mathcal{L}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}

=

+\frac{\mathrm{k}}{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right)^{2}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}+\frac{2}{3}\left(\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\mathcal{W}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}

=

-\frac{\mathrm{k}}{6{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\left[-\frac{8}{9}\left(\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right)^{3}+\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\left(2\left(\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right)^{2}+\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right)+3\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}+\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{2}\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right]

(35)

and

\mathcal{\tilde{L}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}

=

-\frac{\mathrm{k}}{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\left[\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0})^{2}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}+\frac{2}{3}(\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0})^{2}\right]

\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}

=

+\frac{\mathrm{k}}{6{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\left[-\frac{8}{9}(\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0})^{3}+\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left(2(\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0})^{2}+\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right)+3\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}+\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{2}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right]

(36)

These Sugawara like relationships between (
\mathcal{L}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{W}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
) and (
\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0},\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}
) as well as the ones between their right
counterparts (
\mathcal{\tilde{L}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
) and (
\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0},\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}
) are well known in the Literature of the W-algebras extending the 2D boundary conformal invariance; they
are generated by the so-called Miura transformations
[
36
]

of the W
3
-symmetry.

3
Real forms and boundary configurations

In this section, we develop our approach to
deal with the equations of motion (
12
) in order to investigate the
linkage between HS black flowers and the conserved charges of the real forms
of the complexified gauge symmetry
SL\left(3,\mathbb{C}\right)_{L}\times SL\left(3,\mathbb{C}\right)_{R}
. First, we shed

light on
the role of the real forms of the SL(N,
\mathbb{C}
) algebra in the
computation of the entropy
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bf}}}
of the
thermal HS black flowers. Then, we explore various possible solutions of the
equations of motion (
12
) with potentials as in (
28
).

For these purposes, we start by the equations of motion

\partial_{t}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{diag}}}-\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}a_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}

=

0

\partial_{t}\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{diag}}}-\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\tilde{a}_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}

=

0

(37)

having no quadratic term because the diagonal gauge connections commute;
i.e:
[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{diag}}},a_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}]=[\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{diag}}},\tilde{a}_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}]=0
due to the trivial commutation relation
\left[L_{0},W_{0}\right]=0.
Using (
33
), the equations (
37
) split as follows

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\partial_{t}\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}-\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\Upsilon_{t}^{{\small 0}}$&$=$&$0$\\
$\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}+\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\tilde{\Upsilon}_{t}^{{\small 0}}$&$=$&$0$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\partial_{t}\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}-\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\Gamma_{t}^{{\small 0}}$&$=$&$0$\\
$\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}+\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\tilde{\Gamma}_{t}^{{\small 0}}$&$=$&$0$\end{tabular}

(38)

These differential equations give dynamical relationships between the
boundary charges (
\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}},\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}
) and (
\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}},\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}
) of the HS theory
SL\left(3,\mathbb{R}\right)_{L}\times SL\left(3,\mathbb{R}\right)_{R}
and the associated
Lagrange multipliers (
\Upsilon_{t}^{{\small 0}},\Gamma_{t}^{{\small 0}}
)
and (
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{t}^{{\small 0}},\tilde{\Gamma}_{t}^{{\small 0}}
).

There are various methods to solve and work out the properties of the eqs(
37
), among which we cite:

\left(\mathbf{a}\right)

the topological boundary gauge
configuration. Here, the gauge curvatures
f^{\text{{diag}}}=da^{\text{{diag}}}
and
\tilde{f}^{\text{{diag}}}=d\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}
vanish identically (
da^{\text{{diag}}}=d\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}=0);
they correspond to exact boundary
potential forms namely
a^{\text{{diag}}}=d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}
and
\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}=d\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}
. This asymptotic image of the
potentials does not suit the goal of our study and will be accordingly
dropped.

\left(\mathbf{b}\right)

the fixed Lagrange multipliers. This
solution is based on boundary potentials having constrained Lagrange
multipliers like
\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\Upsilon_{t}=\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\Gamma_{t}=0.
These conditions can be effectively fulfilled by just taking
\Upsilon_{t}=
cte
2
and
\Gamma_{t}=
cte
3
; they allow explicit calculations and permit to extract interesting
results without need of involved computations. It will be thoroughly
examined for the remainder of the paper.

\left(\mathbf{c}\right)

the primary scalar and/or fermionic
fields. In this approach, we consider bosonic and fermionic conformal fields
fulfilling boundary field equations of motion given by (
37
). This
two-dimensional QFT model can be viewed as a boundary CFT
2
solution
that permits interesting physical interpretations. For example, it
allows to realise the Heisenberg soft hair states
\left|N_{\text{{soft-hair}}}\right\rangle
at the horizon of the HS- black
flowers
[
9
]
in terms of particles of the HS black flower.
Later on this section, we delve into the key idea behind this proposal which we will further develop in section 5.

3.1
Real forms of complex Lie symmetries

Before going into further details, we

should first
shed

light on the significant role played by the real
forms of the complex SL(N,
\mathbb{C}
) in the derivation of the entropy

S
{}_{\text{{hs-bf}}}
of the thermal HS black flowers. Indeed, in order to calculate the entropies
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bf}}}
of the thermal HS- black holes, one needs to
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
compactify the time variable by performing a Wick rotation
t=it_{E}
parameterising the thermal cycle
\mathcal{C}_{t_{{\small E}}}=\left[0,\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right]
with
\textstyle\mathchar 268
referring to the
inverse of temperature; and
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
demand regularity
of the holonomy

\exp\left(i\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\mathcal{C}_{t_{{\small E}}}}a_{t_{{\small E}}}dt_{E}\right)

(39)

that must be equal to
(-)^{N+1}I_{id}
due to the reality of the gauge
symmetry SL(N,
\mathbb{R}).
In this regard, notice that for odd values of
N, this holonomy must be equal to the identity
I_{id}
(termed below as
trivial holonomy or neutral holonomy because
I_{id}
is the neutral element
of
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
) while for even
N
, it is equal to
-I_{id}.
More
generally, for the complexified SL(N,
\mathbb{C})
, one may replace this
condition by
zI_{id}
where the complex number z is constrained like
z^{N}=1
having N solutions given by
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}=e^{2i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}p/N}

corresponding to the group elements of the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
centre of SL(N,
\mathbb{C}).
For odd N, we have only one real solution
I_{id}
while for
even N we get both
\pm I_{id}
. As for the complex solutions, they depend
on the rational
p/N
numbers associated to the phase
2p{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}/N
beyond the
values 0 and
\textstyle\mathchar 281

\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits 2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}
.

Moreover, the Wick rotation
t=it_{E}
maps the boundary cylinder
\mathcal{C}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
parameterised by (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t
) to the
torus
\mathbb{T}^{2}=\mathcal{C}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\times\mathcal{C}_{t_{E}}

coordinated by the periodic variables (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{E}
). Furthermore, under
time compactification we have the following features:

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)

the
real
partial connections
a_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}dt=\Upsilon+\Gamma
and
\tilde{a}_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}dt=\tilde{\Upsilon}+\Gamma
valued in sl(3,
\mathbb{R}
)
with L
0
and W
0
components as

\begin{tabular}[]{ll}$\Upsilon$&$=\left(\Upsilon_{t}^{0}L_{0}\right)dt$\\
$\tilde{\Upsilon}$&$=(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{t}^{0}L_{0})dt$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{ll}$\Gamma$&$=\left(\Gamma_{t}^{0}W_{0}\right)dt$\\
$\tilde{\Gamma}$&$=(\tilde{\Gamma}_{t}^{0}W_{0})dt$\end{tabular}

(40)

get mapped to the
pure imaginary
ones

\begin{tabular}[]{ll}$i\Upsilon_{E}$&$=\Upsilon_{t_{E}}^{0}\left(iL_{0}\right)dt_{E}$\\
$i\tilde{\Upsilon}_{E}$&$=\tilde{\Upsilon}_{t_{E}}^{0}(iL_{0})dt_{E}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{ll}$i\Gamma_{E}$&$=\Gamma_{t_{E}}^{0}\left(iW_{0}\right)dt_{E}$\\
$i\tilde{\Gamma}_{E}$&$=\tilde{\Gamma}_{t_{E}}^{0}(iW_{0})dt_{E}$\end{tabular}

(41)

which no longer sit in the real sl(3,
\mathbb{R}
).

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)

the real exponentiations of (
40
) valued in the real group SL(3,
\mathbb{R}
) namely

\begin{tabular}[]{ll}$\exp(\int\Upsilon)$&$=\exp\left(\int\left(\Upsilon_{t}^{0}L_{0}\right)dt\right)$\\
$\exp(\int\tilde{\Upsilon})$&$=\exp\left(\int(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{t}^{0}L_{0})dt\right)$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{ll}$\exp(\int\Gamma)$&$=\exp\left(\int\left(\Gamma_{t}^{0}W_{0}\right)dt\right)$\\
$\exp(\int\tilde{\Gamma})$&$=\exp\left(\int(\tilde{\Gamma}_{t}^{0}W_{0})dt\right)$\end{tabular},

(42)

get in turn mapped into the following complex U(1) phases

\begin{tabular}[]{ll}$\exp(i\int\Upsilon_{E})$&$=\exp\left(i\int\left(\Upsilon_{t_{E}}^{0}L_{0}\right)dt_{E}\right)$\\
$\exp(i\int\tilde{\Upsilon}_{E})$&$=\exp\left(i\int(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{t_{E}}^{0}L_{0})dt_{E}\right)$\end{tabular},\quad\begin{tabular}[]{ll}$\exp(i\int\Gamma_{E})$&$=\exp\left(i\int\left(\Gamma_{t_{E}}^{0}W_{0}\right)dt_{E}\right)$\\
$\exp(i\int\tilde{\Gamma}_{E})$&$=\exp\left(i\int(\tilde{\Gamma}_{t_{E}}^{0}W_{0})dt_{E}\right)$\end{tabular}

(43)

These mappings show that under Wick rotation, the real matrices (
L_{0},W_{0}
) are replaced by the
pure imaginary
(
iL_{0},iW_{0}
).
Notice that the real (
L_{0},W_{0}
) generates the real abelian subgroup
S\left[GL(1,\mathbb{R})\times GL(1,\mathbb{R})\right]
of SL(3,
\mathbb{R}
) with group elements as

g_{{}_{{\small SL}_{{\small 3}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}e^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}^{0}L_{0}}&0&0\\
0&e^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{0}W_{0}}&0\\
0&0&e^{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}^{0}L_{0}}e^{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{0}W_{0}}\end{array}\right),\qquad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}^{0},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{0}\in\mathbb{R},\qquad g_{{}_{{\small SL}_{{\small 3}}}}^{\ast}=g_{{}_{{\small SL}_{{\small 3}}}}^{\dagger}=g_{{}_{{\small SL}_{{\small 3}}}},

(44)

However, the pure imaginary (
iL_{0},iW_{0}
) engender the complex unitary
S\left[U(1)\times U(1)\right]
subgroup of SU(3) with elements as

g_{{}_{{\small SU}_{{\small 3}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}e^{i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}^{0}L_{0}}&0&0\\
0&e^{i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{0}W_{0}}&0\\
0&0&e^{-i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}^{0}L_{0}}e^{-i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{0}W_{0}}\end{array}\right),\qquad g_{{}_{{\small SU}_{{\small 3}}}}^{\ast}=g_{{}_{{\small SU}_{{\small 3}}}}^{\dagger}=g_{{}_{{\small SU}_{{\small 3}}}}^{-1}

(45)

So, under the Wick rotation, the real SL(3,
\mathbb{R}
) gets mapped into
the complex unitary SU(3). This relationship between SL(3,
\mathbb{R}
) and
SU(3) is not unusual considering that they are, respectively, the split and
compact real forms of SL(3,
\mathbb{C}
). This feature indicates that the
real forms of SL(3,
\mathbb{C}
) should play an important role in
the investigation of black hole thermodynamics. As such, the
various real forms of SL(N,
\mathbb{C}
) have to be monitored while
computing the HS black holes entropies especially while descending towards
the core spin 2 black holes of AdS
3
gravity. For these
reasons, we briefly overview the real forms of the
SL(N,\mathbb{C})
series with concrete examples and graphical illustrations given
by Tits-Satake diagrams
[
37
,
38
]
in appendix A.

3.2
Solving the equations of motion

Since the equations of motion (
38
) play an important role in the
study of HS black holes, we find it imperative to explore the various
possible solutions. Strictly speaking, the boundary (
a^{\text{{diag}}},\tilde{a}^{\text{{diag}}}
) involved in these relations are not
arbitrary fields seeing that they must obey (
38
) and undergo certain
gauge transformations. Thus, the fundamental variables are a priori obtained
by solving the field equations (
38
); which can be achieved in various
ways depending on the targeted features. Below, we give three types of
solutions: the first two are described in details here after while the third
is briefly addressed as it will be further investigated in section 5.

3.2.1
Topological gauge configuration

The first type of

(
38
)’s solutions is given by the
trivial realisation with boundary potentials taken as follows

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}$&$=$&$\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}_{{\small 1}}^{{\small 0}}$\\
$\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}$&$=$&$\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}_{{\small 2}}^{{\small 0}}$\end{tabular},\quad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\Upsilon_{t}^{{\small 0}}$&$=$&$\partial_{t}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}_{{\small 1}}^{{\small 0}}$\\
