{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{N}^{0}=\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}N.

For the case
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}=0
, we have

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}e^{i\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\frac{2}{3}\Lambda^{0}}&0&0\\
0&e^{-i\frac{4}{3}\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\Lambda^{0}}&0\\
0&0&e^{i\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\frac{2}{3}\Lambda^{0}}\end{array}\right)=e^{i2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}p/3}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}e^{i2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}N}&0&0\\
0&e^{-i2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{\small M}}&0\\
0&0&e^{i2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}N}\end{array}\right)

(75)

giving

\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{ccc}$\frac{2}{3}\Lambda^{0}$&$=$&$\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\left(N+\frac{1}{3}p\right)$\\
$\frac{4}{3}\Lambda^{0}$&$=$&$\frac{2{\small{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\left(M+\frac{2}{3}p\right)$\end{tabular}\right.\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\Lambda^{0}=\frac{3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\left(N+\frac{1}{3}p\right)

(76)

solved by
M=2N
and then
\Lambda^{0}=\frac{3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\left(N+\frac{1}{3}p\right)
can be written like
\Lambda_{N,p}^{0}=\frac{3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\left(N+\frac{1}{3}p\right).

B)

Eqs(
38
) from a variational
principle and BH entropy

Here, we go over the variational principle leading to the relations (
38
) in preparation for the derivation of our
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}
formula for the entropy of HS black flowers. As a follow
up, we compare the well- known
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}},\
calculated by GPPTT (
Grumiller et al
)
in
[
6
]
[see eq(4.5) there], with our S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}
and comment on the conserved quantities for this
family of HS black holes.

B.1)

Variational principle

Using the hypothesis (
48
), the field equations (
38
) reduce
down to the remarkable relations

\begin{tabular}[]{lllll}$\partial_{t}\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}$&$=$&$\left(\partial_{t}\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right)L_{0}$&$=$&$0$\\
$\partial_{t}\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}$&$=$&$\left(\partial_{t}\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0}\right)W_{0}$&$=$&$0$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lllll}$\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}$&$=$&$(\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0})L_{0}$&$=$&$0$\\
$\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}$&$=$&$(\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{0})W_{0}$&$=$&$0$\end{tabular}

(77)

These relations can be interpreted as 2D field equations of motion
descending from a 2D action principle as follows

\displaystyle\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}\mathcal{H}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\qquad,\qquad\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}\mathcal{H}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}

\displaystyle\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}\mathcal{H}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}\Lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\qquad,\qquad\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}\mathcal{H}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}\tilde{\Lambda}}=\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}

(78)

here the (
\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
) and (
\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
) are functions of the variables (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t
); i.e:

\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t\right)\quad,\quad\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t\right)\quad,\quad\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t\right)\quad,\quad\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t\right)

(79)

we find that the action
\mathcal{H}
is a linear combination of two objects

\mathtt{I}
and
\mathtt{\tilde{I}}
such that
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\mathcal{H}=\mathtt{I}+\mathtt{\tilde{I}}
where

\displaystyle 2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathtt{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{k}}{4}\int_{0}^{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}dt\left(\int_{0}^{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}Tr\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\partial_{t}\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}+\Lambda\partial_{t}\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle 2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathtt{\tilde{I}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{k}}{4}\int_{0}^{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}dt\left(\int_{0}^{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}Tr\left[\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}+\tilde{\Lambda}\partial_{t}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\right]\right)

(80)

with Lagrange multipliers (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}},\Lambda
) and (
\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}},\tilde{\Lambda}
) being constant parameters as previously chosen. An
alternative approach to derive (
77
) is to consider instead of
H
the
quantity
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\mathcal{P}=\mathtt{I}-\mathtt{\tilde{I}}
defining
the angular momentum.

B.2)

Deriving BH entropy

We show below that the absolute values
\left|\mathtt{I}\right|

and
|\mathtt{\tilde{I}}|
are

closely related to the GPPTT entropy
{S}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}
given by,

\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(2n+m\right)\left(J_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}\right)+3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}m\left(K_{0}+\bar{K}_{0}\right)

(81)

with an affiliation to the boundary hamiltonian given by the variation
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}=\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}\mathcal{H}^{\text{{gpptt}}}\right)
.
In what follows, we establish that:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)

the entropy
{S}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}
denotes a particular layer of the
black flower solution of higher spin gravity with symmetry
SU(3)_{{\small L}}\times SU(3)_{{\small R}}.
It is associated with the neutral holonomy
\{I_{id}\}
sitting in the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}=\{I_{id},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{2}\}

centre

of
SU(3)
.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

the eq (
81
) corresponds
to the maximal value of

two gauge invariants

\mathtt{I}^{\pm}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left|\mathtt{I}\pm\mathtt{\tilde{I}}\right|

(82)

where the left sector charge (and equivalently the right) is given by
\mathtt{I}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathfrak{N}J_{0}+3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathfrak{M}K_{0}
with
\mathfrak{N}
and
\mathfrak{M}
to be determined. Due to the inequality
|\mathtt{I}\pm\mathtt{\tilde{I}}|

\leq

|\mathtt{I}|+|\mathtt{\tilde{I}}|,
the invariants (
82
) are upper bounded by the maximal value

\text{{{S}}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(\left|\mathtt{I}\right|+|\mathtt{\tilde{I}}|\right)

(83)

defining the entropy of the HS-BF.

To prove that
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}
comprises

S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}},
we must
calculate the integrals in eq(
80
). However, this requires a certain
knowledge of the dependencies of (
\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
) and (
\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
) on the Euclidean time
t=it_{{\small E}}
and the angular variable
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}.
In fact, in addition to
the Lagrange multipliers being constants in our description, we can impose a
reliance on the Euclidean time in (
79
) as follows

\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}\right)

=

-\frac{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}{2}\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)\cos({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\frac{t_{{\small E}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}})

\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}\right)

=

-\frac{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}{2}\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)\cos({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\frac{t_{{\small E}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}})

\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}\right)

=

-\frac{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}{2}\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)\cos({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\frac{t_{{\small E}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}})

\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}\right)

=

-\frac{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}{2}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)\cos({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\frac{t_{{\small E}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}})

(84)

with
t_{{\small E}}
varying in the 1-cycle
\left[0,\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right].
Notice that the above factorisation of the variables (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}
) follows from the equations of motion (
77
) demanding the
equality of the time derivatives (velocities) of these relations at
t_{{\small E}}=0
and

B.3)

Comments on
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}

and

S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}

entropies

Our generalised solution is given by the maximal value
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}(\left|\mathtt{I}\right|+|\mathtt{\tilde{I}}|)
; it reads for the compact real form
SU(3)
of the
complexified gauge symmetry as follows

\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left|\mathfrak{N}\right|\left(\left|J_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}}\right|+|\tilde{J}_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}}|\right)+3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left|\mathfrak{M}\right|\left(\left|K_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}}\right|+|\tilde{K}_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}}|\right)

(92)

where
(\left|J_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}}\right|,|K_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}}|)
and
(|\tilde{J}_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}}|,|\tilde{K}_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}})
[ for short (
\left|J_{0}\right|,|K_{0}|
) and (
|\tilde{J}_{0}|,|\tilde{K}_{0}
)] are the absolute value
of the zero modes of conserved
\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}
Û(1)
k
currents.
This entropy can be also presented as

S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}

=

2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left|n+\frac{1}{2}m\right|\left(J_{0}+\tilde{J}_{0}\right)+

3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left|\frac{1}{2}m+\frac{1}{3}p\right|\left(K_{0}+\tilde{K}_{0}\right)

(93)

The number n, m are integers (
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}
) with
p
belonging to
\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}.
This reformulation of the expression helps to
expose several features:

•

Case

p=0

mod

3
:

With this parametrisation, the higher spin black hole entropy relation
coincides with the entropy
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}
given by
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(2n+m\right)\left(J_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}\right)+

3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}m\left(K_{0}+\bar{K}_{0}\right).
Because
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}>0,
we can assure the
fulfillment of this condition by taking
n
and
m
to be positive integers
with
J_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}>0;
that is
\mathop{\mathrm{R}e}\nolimits(J_{0})>0
and
\mathop{\mathrm{R}e}\nolimits(K_{0})>0
. Additionally, by setting
m=0,
the GPPTT reduces to

\left.\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}\right|_{m=0}=2n{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(J_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}\right)

(94)

recuperating therefore the spin 2 black holes characterised by the even
integer
2n
. As we will see below [see eq(
97
)], this even parity is
closely related to the identity element in the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\{I_{id},-I_{id}\}
group centre of
SU(2).

•

Case

p\neq 0

mod 3 and m=0
:

By setting
m=0
, the BH entropy (
93
) reads as follows

\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}n\left(J_{0}+\bar{J}_{0}\right)+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}|p|\left(K_{0}+\bar{K}_{0}\right)

(95)

we therefore have

\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}=\left.\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}\right|_{m=0}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}|p|\left(K_{0}+\bar{K}_{0}\right)

(96)

indicating that for non trivial holonomies, the branch m=0 has a
contribution stemming from the spin s=3 fields. As such, for
p\neq 0
mod
3, one cannot continuously go from the entropy
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}
of the HS black flowers towards the entropy of the spin 2 black
hole; a leap is required as depicted by Figure
2
.

Figure 2:
The different branches joining the higher spin s=3 black flowers to
the spin s=2 black holes carrying SU
\left(2\right)
symmetry. Except for
trivial holonomies, the descent is given by discrete transitions showcasing the fact that
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is not a subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
.

•

Case of spin s=2 gravity

Here, the generators of sl(2,
\mathbb{C}
) are in the isospin j=1/2, they
are given by the 2
\times
2 matrices (
20
). The centre of sl(2,
\mathbb{C}
) is given by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\{I_{id},-I_{id}\}

and is
represented by the two layers of Figure
3
.

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
of the compact real form SU(2) of the spin

for z=1 and

for

The regularity of the thermal holonomy reads in general as follows

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}e^{i\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}}&0\\
0&e^{-i\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}e^{i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}N}&0\\

(97)

where

is an integer solved as
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}N
. For
even integer

3.2.3
Using primary scalars and fermions

The third approach to work out the solutions of eq(
38
)
exploits primary conformal field theory realisations to build
effective 2D conformal field models while assuming the conditions
\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\Upsilon_{t}^{0}=\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\Gamma_{t}^{0}=0
and

\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}_{t}^{0}=\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{t}^{0}=0
. Here we think about the boundary potentials
in terms of scalar and/or fermi fields with conformal stress tensor realised
in terms of oscillating fields. In this regard, notice that for the spin 2
gravity model, the affine symmetry in the AdS
3
boundary surface can be
either the compact real form SU(2)

or the non compact SL(2,
\mathbb{R}
)
k
depending on the considered gauge symmetry of
the CS model. These boundary symmetries are known to have different bosonic
and fermionic realisations
[
42

43

44
]
; a characteristic
property shared by all real forms of the SL(N,
\mathbb{C}
)
k

family.

These realisations assume the existence of realistic quantum

4
Boundary current algebras and HS-BF entropy

We first investigate the current algebras of the underlying
higher spin symmetries of the previous black flowers with generalised
boundary conditions (
5
) in connection with the real forms of the
complexified sl(N,
\mathbb{C}
). Then, we construct the quantum states
making the HS soft Heisenberg hair of these black holes. We conclude this
section with explicit entropy computations of the various higher spin black
flowers. The analysis will mainly focus on the higher spins s=2,3 black
flowers, yet it can be seamlessly generalised.

4.1
Current algebra and sl(3,
\mathbb{C}
) real forms

We begin by recalling that the conserved currents (
\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
) and (
\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
) given by the expansions (
252
) generate an
infinite dimensional symmetry on the 2D boundary gauge theory. Pertaining to
the case of real forms of the affine
\widehat{sl}(3,\mathbb{C})_{L}\times\widehat{sl}(3,\mathbb{C})_{R}
, the 2D invariance consists of 2+2
isomorphic infinite dimensional Heisenberg symmetries namely: the 2 left
sector sub-symmetries
\hat{u}(1)_{\mathcal{J}}\times\hat{u}(1)_{\mathcal{K}}
of the affine
\widehat{su}(3,\mathbb{C})_{L}
and the 2 right sector
\hat{u}(1)_{\mathcal{\tilde{J}}}\times\hat{u}(1)_{\mathcal{\tilde{K}}}

contained in the affine
\widehat{su}(3,\mathbb{C})_{R}.

In this regard, the vanishing property of the left partial charge potential
da^{\text{{diag}}}=\mathcal{J}^{{\small 0}}L_{0}+\mathcal{K}^{{\small 0}}W_{0}=0
with periodic
\mathcal{J}^{{\small 0}}=\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}
and
\mathcal{K}^{{\small 0}}=\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}
shows that the currents
\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}
and
\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}
are conserved (
\partial_{t}\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}=\partial_{t}\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}=0
). And due to their periodicity property, they
expand in Fourier modes as
\sum J_{n}^{0}e^{-in{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
and
\sum K_{n}^{0}e^{-in{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
inducing two infinite series of conserved charges
{
J_{n}^{0}
} and {
K_{n}^{0}
}. Therefore,
\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}
and
\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{{\small 0}}
generate the
following current algebras (affine Kac-Moody for quantum) written for the

\widehat{sl}(3,\mathbb{C})_{L}

q_{2}\left|q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

,\qquad

\boldsymbol{a}_{{\small+n}}\left|q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

=

0

\boldsymbol{b}_{{\small 0}}\left|q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

=

q_{3}\left|q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

,\qquad

\boldsymbol{b}_{{\small+n}}\left|q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

=

0

(115)

where
q_{2}
and
q_{3}
are integers. The excited states generating the
two HW representations are built per usual as follows

\displaystyle\left|N_{0};q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\text{{norm}}}\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{i=1}^{N_{\mathcal{J}}}\boldsymbol{a}_{n_{i}}^{{\small\dagger}}\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{j=1}^{N_{\mathcal{K}}}\boldsymbol{b}_{m_{j}}^{\dagger}\left|q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

(116)

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{N}_{0};\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\text{{norm}}}\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{i=1}^{N_{\mathcal{J}}}\boldsymbol{\tilde{a}}_{n_{i}}^{{\small\dagger}}\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{j=1}^{N_{\mathcal{K}}}\boldsymbol{\tilde{b}}_{m_{j}}^{\dagger}\left|\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{3}\right\rangle

(117)

with normalisations
\mathcal{N}_{\text{{norm}}}
and excitation
numbers

N_{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\mathcal{J}}}n_{i}\qquad,\qquad\tilde{N}_{0}=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\mathcal{K}}}m_{j}

(118)

Because of the commutation relations (
113
), all the excited states of
the above HW representations have the same charges under
\boldsymbol{a}_{{\small 0}}=\boldsymbol{J}_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}}
and
\boldsymbol{b}_{{\small 0}}=\boldsymbol{K}_{{\small 0}}^{{\small 0}}
, that is

\boldsymbol{a}_{{\small 0}}\left|N_{0};q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

=

q_{2}\left|N_{0};q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

\boldsymbol{b}_{{\small 0}}\left|N_{0};q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

=

q_{3}\left|N_{0};q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

(119)

with mean values

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left\langle N_{0}\right|\boldsymbol{a}_{{\small 0}}\left|N_{0}\right\rangle$&$=$&$q_{2}$\\
$\left\langle N_{0}\right|\boldsymbol{b}_{{\small 0}}\left|N_{0}\right\rangle$&$=$&$q_{3}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left\langle\tilde{N}_{0}\right|\boldsymbol{\tilde{a}}_{{\small 0}}\left|\tilde{N}_{0}\right\rangle$&$=$&$\tilde{q}_{2}$\\
$\left\langle\tilde{N}_{0}\right|\boldsymbol{\tilde{b}}_{{\small 0}}\left|\tilde{N}_{0}\right\rangle$&$=$&$\tilde{q}_{3}$\end{tabular}

(120)

These quantum states define the soft Heisenberg hair states of the HS black
holes (black flowers)
[
5
,
6
]
, they are characterised by the
charges
q_{2},q_{3}
and
\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{3}

independently of
\boldsymbol{J}_{n}^{{\small 0}}
and
\boldsymbol{K}_{n}^{{\small 0}}
.

4.1.2
Conformal weights of black flowers

The Fourier modes (
\boldsymbol{J}_{n}^{{\small 0}},\boldsymbol{K}_{n}^{{\small 0}}
) and (
\boldsymbol{\tilde{J}}_{n}^{{\small 0}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{K}}_{n}^{{\small 0}}
) are eigenvalues of the scale operators
\mathfrak{L}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{\tilde{L}}_{0}
of the Virasoro algebras

\left[\mathfrak{L}_{n},\mathfrak{L}_{m}\right]

=

\left(n-m\right)\mathfrak{L}_{n+m}+\frac{c_{L}}{12}\left(n^{3}-n\right){{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{n+m,0}

\left[\mathfrak{\tilde{L}}_{n},\mathfrak{\tilde{L}}_{m}\right]

=

\left(n-m\right)\mathfrak{\tilde{L}}_{n+m}+\frac{c_{R}}{12}\left(n^{3}-n\right){{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{n+m,0}

(121)

with
\mathfrak{L}_{n}=\int_{0}^{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}e^{in{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\mathcal{L}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}/(2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}})
with
\mathcal{L}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
as in (
35
).
We have

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left[\mathfrak{L}_{0},\boldsymbol{J}_{n}^{{\small 0}}\right]$&$=$&$-n\boldsymbol{J}_{n}^{{\small 0}}$\\
$\left[\mathfrak{L}_{0},\boldsymbol{K}_{n}^{{\small 0}}\right]$&$=$&$-n\boldsymbol{K}_{n}^{{\small 0}}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$[\mathfrak{\tilde{L}}_{0},\boldsymbol{\tilde{J}}_{n}^{{\small 0}}]$&$=$&$-n\boldsymbol{\tilde{J}}_{n}^{{\small 0}}$\\
$[\mathfrak{\tilde{L}}_{0},\boldsymbol{\tilde{K}}_{n}^{{\small 0}}]$&$=$&$-n\boldsymbol{\tilde{K}}_{n}^{{\small 0}}$\end{tabular}

(122)

By using the change
\boldsymbol{a}_{n}=\sqrt{2/\left(\mathrm{k}n\right)}\boldsymbol{J}_{n}^{{\small 0}}
and
\boldsymbol{b}_{n}=\sqrt{2/\left(\mathrm{k}n\right)}\boldsymbol{K}_{n}^{{\small 0}}
, the above relations
read as
\left[\mathfrak{L}_{0},\boldsymbol{a}_{n}\right]=-n\boldsymbol{a}_{n}
and
\left[\mathfrak{L}_{0},\boldsymbol{a}_{n}^{\dagger}\right]=n\boldsymbol{a}_{n}^{\dagger}.
Similar formulas can be written for the
modes
(\boldsymbol{b}_{n},\boldsymbol{b}_{n}^{\dagger})
as well as for
the twild partners. These eigenvalue equations can be solved in terms of the
oscillator numbers
\boldsymbol{a}_{n}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{a}_{n}
,
\boldsymbol{b}_{n}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{b}_{n}
and
\boldsymbol{\tilde{a}}_{n}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\tilde{a}}_{n}
,
\boldsymbol{\tilde{b}}_{n}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\tilde{b}}_{n}
like
\mathfrak{L}_{0}=\sum n(\boldsymbol{a}_{n}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{a}_{n}+\boldsymbol{b}_{n}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{b}_{n})
and
\mathfrak{\tilde{L}}_{0}=\sum n(\boldsymbol{\tilde{a}}_{n}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\tilde{a}}_{n}+\boldsymbol{\tilde{b}}_{n}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\tilde{b}}_{n})
.

In the left sector, the quantum states
\left|q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle

given by the highest weight state representations (
115
) are also
designated by the central charge c
L
and the conformal weight
h
. The
soft Heisenberg hair states are then distinguished by their central charge
and their conformal scales as

\mathfrak{L}_{0}\left|N_{0};q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle=h\left|N_{0};q_{2},q_{3}\right\rangle\qquad,\qquad\mathfrak{L}_{0}\left|\tilde{N}_{0};\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{3}\right\rangle=\tilde{h}\left|\tilde{N}_{0};\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{3}\right\rangle

(123)

with conformal weights

h=N_{0}=\sum_{i}n_{i}\qquad,\qquad\tilde{h}=\tilde{N}_{0}=\sum_{j}m_{j}

(124)

5
Scalar and Fermi fields realisations

In this section, we develop the free field realisations (
103
-
105
) by conceiving the boundary potentials (
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{diag}}},a_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}
) and (
\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{diag}}},\tilde{a}_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}
)
as conformal primary fields with quantum excitations.
Here, the physical charge potentials are imagined as realistic 2D
scalars and/or 2D Fermi fields sitting at the boundary of AdS
3
; and
then at the horizon of the HS black holes. Because the boundary charge
potentials

(\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}})\qquad,\qquad(\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}})

(131)

are affine Heisenberg conserved currents, they carry conformal weights and
so they can be concretely realised in terms of primary conformal
fields. We first investigate the bosonic realisation (
103
),
then we turn to the fermionic one (
105
).

5.1
Charge potentials as primary scalars

Here, the conserved currents (
\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
) and (
\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},

\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}
)
are imagined in terms of two real 2D scalar fields
\Phi
and
\Pi
living
on the boundary of AdS
3
and splitting into left and right moving fields
as

\Phi=\Phi_{L}+\Phi_{R}\qquad,\qquad\Pi=\Pi_{L}+\Pi_{R}

(132)

Generally speaking, these fields are functions of the coordinate variables
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}^{\pm}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}\pm t
namely
\Phi_{L}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}^{+}\right)
,
\Phi_{R}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}^{-}\right)
,
\Pi_{L}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}^{+}\right)
and
\Pi_{R}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}^{-}\right)
. Under the Wick rotation
t=it_{E},
the
hyperbolic coordinates
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}^{\pm}
become complex variables
z^{\pm}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}\pm it_{E}.
Consequently, the left and right chiralities get
mapped into holomorphic
\Phi_{L}\left(z\right)
(resp.
\Pi_{L}\left(z\right)
) and antiholomorphic
\Phi_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)
(resp.
\Pi_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)
). The non vanishing two-points correlations
of these 2D scalar fields are given by

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left\langle\Phi_{L}\left(z\right)\Phi_{L}\left(w\right)\right\rangle$&$\sim$&$\log\left(z-w\right)$\\
$\left\langle\Phi_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)\Phi_{R}\left(\bar{w}\right)\right\rangle$&$\sim$&$\log\left(\bar{z}-\bar{w}\right)$\end{tabular},\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left\langle\Pi_{L}\left(z\right)\Pi_{L}\left(w\right)\right\rangle$&$\sim$&$\log\left(z-w\right)$\\
$\left\langle\Pi_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)\Pi_{R}\left(\bar{w}\right)\right\rangle$&$\sim$&$\log\left(\bar{z}-\bar{w}\right)$\end{tabular}

(133)

from which we deduce other two-points correlations, in particular

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left\langle\mathcal{J}_{L}\left(z\right)\Phi_{L}\left(w\right)\right\rangle$&$\sim$&$\frac{1}{z-w}$\\
$\left\langle\mathcal{J}_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)\Phi_{R}\left(\bar{w}\right)\right\rangle$&$\sim$&$\frac{1}{\bar{z}-\bar{w}}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{L}\left(z\right)\Pi_{L}\left(w\right)\right\rangle$&$\sim$&$\frac{1}{z-w}$\\
$\left\langle\mathcal{K}_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)\Pi_{R}\left(\bar{w}\right)\right\rangle$&$\sim$&$\frac{1}{\bar{z}-\bar{w}}$\end{tabular}

(134)

where we have set

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathcal{J}_{L}\left(z\right)$&$=$&$\frac{\partial\Phi_{L}\left(z\right)}{\partial z}$\\
$\mathcal{K}_{L}\left(z\right)$&$=$&$\frac{\partial\Pi_{L}\left(z\right)}{\partial z}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathcal{J}_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)$&$=$&$\frac{\partial\Phi_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)}{\partial\bar{z}}$\\
$\mathcal{K}_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)$&$=$&$\frac{\partial\Pi_{R}\left(\bar{z}\right)}{\partial\bar{z}}$\end{tabular}

(135)

Notice that the commutation relations (
111
) can be also formulated as OPE with z-complex coordinate variable as follows
[

from which we learn the expression of the zero modes

$\boldsymbol{K}_{0}$&$=$&$\frac{3}{2\mathrm{k}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}:\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}_{-m}^{+}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}_{m}^{-}:$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\boldsymbol{\tilde{J}}_{0}$&$=$&$\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}:\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}}}_{-m}^{+}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}}}_{m}^{-}:$\\

(185)

with
\left\langle N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}},N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}|\boldsymbol{J}_{0}|N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}},N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}\right\rangle=N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}}
and
\left\langle N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}},N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}|\boldsymbol{K}_{0}|N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}},N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}\right\rangle=N_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}
and higher spin black
hole entropies as in eq(
179
).

6
Conclusion and Discussions

To bring this paper to a close, we summarise our main results
along with a few additional comments. Setting out with higher spin black
flowers solutions of

3D Anti-de Sitter gravity with
\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
generalised boundary conditions as in eq(
5
) and (
\mathbf{2}
) gauge symmetry defined by real forms of the complexified
SL(N,\mathbb{C})_{\mathtt{L}}\times SL(N,\mathbb{C})_{\mathtt{R}}
group, we considered non
trivial holonomies
W_{\left[\mathcal{C}_{t}\right]}=P\exp(i\int_{\mathcal{C}_{t}}a)

taken values in the discrete group
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
with boundary potentials circling the thermal cycle
\left[\mathcal{C}_{t}\right].
We showed that these HS black flowers are in fact

of
various types

depending on the selected real form of the complex
SL(N,\mathbb{C})\
whether it is the real split
SL(N,\mathbb{R}),
the
compact
SU(N)
or one of the several non compact
SU(N_{1},N_{2})
. We also
characterised the BH flowers by two infinite sets of conserved charges, one
left
\mathtt{I}_{r}^{\text{{l}}}
and another right
\mathtt{I}_{r}^{\text{{r}}}
(
r\in\mathbb{Z}
), obeying an affine Heisenberg- like
algebra. These (left/right) gauge invariants
\mathtt{I}_{r}
expand like
\sum_{s=2}^{N}\Lambda^{{\small(s-1)}}\mathcal{W}_{r}^{{\small(s)}}
where
the
\Lambda^{{\small(s-1)}}
are real Lagrange multipliers and where
\mathcal{W}_{r}^{{\small(s)}}

are the
(N-1)
affine GL(1)
k
charges of the underlying affine
SL(N)_{\mathrm{k}}
. Focusing
on the compact
SU(N)
theory [similar discussion follows for the
SU(N_{1},N_{2})
] and demanding non trivial holonomies sitting in the centre
of the gauge symmetry, the conserved charges
\mathtt{I}_{r}
as well as the
associated Lagrange multipliers
\Lambda_{n_{s},p_{N}}^{{\small(s-1)}}

become quantized values with
n_{s}\in\mathbb{Z}
parametrising higher
spins and an additional charges
p_{N}=0,...,N-1

\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits N
sweeping the
N group elements of the group centre
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
. The additional
charges index gauge invariant physical states that we have interpreted in
terms of layered HS black hole flowers. For an in-depth discussion
on the physical significance of this new sector arising from the centre
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
symmetry, consult
Appendix C
for more details.

Furthermore, by borrowing ideas from constrained Hamiltonian systems, we
\left(\mathbf{a}\right)
developed an approach to address the vanishing
condition of the gauge curvature on the boundary surface of the AdS
3

space in order to compute the conserved
\mathtt{I}_{r}\left[\Lambda\right];
and
\left(\mathbf{b}\right)
ascertained that the HS black flowers
entropy is intimately related to the invariants

\mathcal{I}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{+}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}|\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{l}}}+\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{r}}}|\qquad,\qquad\mathcal{I}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{-}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}|\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{l}}}-\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{r}}}|

(186)

via the upper bound given by

\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{N}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}|\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{l}}}|+2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}|\mathtt{I}_{0}^{\text{{r}}}|

(187)

Assuming that
\Lambda_{n_{s},p}^{\text{{l}}}=\Lambda_{n_{s},p_{N}}^{\text{{r}}}

\geq 0
, the entropy
S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{N}}
can be factorised like

\text{{\cal S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{2}}

=

2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{n_{2},p_{2}}\left(\left|\mathcal{W}_{0}^{{\small(2)}}\right|+\left|\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{0}^{{\small(2)}}\right|\right)

\text{{\cal S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{3}}

=

2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\Lambda_{n_{2},p_{2}}\left(\left|\mathcal{W}_{0}^{{\small(2)}}\right|+\left|\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{0}^{{\small(2)}}\right|\right)+3{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\Lambda_{n_{3},p_{3}}\left(\left|\mathcal{W}_{0}^{{\small(3)}}\right|+\left|\mathcal{\tilde{W}}_{0}^{{\small(3)}}\right|\right)

(188)

with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{n_{2},p_{2}}=n_{2}+p_{2}/2
for the SU
2
theory and
\Lambda_{n_{2},p_{2}}=n_{2}+n_{3}/2
as well as
\Lambda_{n_{3},p_{3}}=n_{3}/2+p_{3}/3
for SU
3
. This entropy comprises the
familiar Grumiller- Perez- Prohazka- Tempo- Troncoso (GPPTT) entropy S
{}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{gpptt}}}
as a particular phase
associated with the trivial holonomy where
p_{{\small N}}=0
mod N.

At the quantum level, the classical invariants
\mathtt{I}_{0}
and
\mathtt{\tilde{I}}_{0}
get promoted to operators denoted like
\boldsymbol{I}_{0}=\sum_{s=2}^{N}\Lambda_{\text{{l}}}^{{\small(s-1)}}\boldsymbol{W}_{0}^{{\small(s)}}
and
\boldsymbol{\tilde{I}}_{0}=\sum_{s=2}^{N}\Lambda_{\text{{r}}}^{{\small(s-1)}}\boldsymbol{\tilde{W}}_{0}^{{\small(s)}}\
implying an interpretation of the HS-BF in terms of the vacuum
expectation value. The operator
\boldsymbol{I}_{0}
(similarly for
\boldsymbol{\tilde{I}}_{0}
) is taken in the Cartan subalgebra of SL(N)
0

which is the zero mode of the affine SL(N)
k
. Thus, given a
highest weight representation state
\left|\mathbf{q}\right\rangle
of
SL(N)
0
labelled by the
N-1
charges like
\left|q_{{\small 2}},...,q_{{\small N}}\right\rangle
with eigenvalue equation
\boldsymbol{W}_{0}^{{\small(s)}}\left|\mathbf{q}\right\rangle=q_{{\small s}}\left|\mathbf{q}\right\rangle
and positive integer
q_{{\small s}},

the invariant
\mathtt{I}_{0}
can be considered as the mean
\left\langle\mathbf{q}\right|\boldsymbol{I}_{0}\left|\mathbf{q}\right\rangle=\sum_{s=2}^{N}q_{{\small s}}\Lambda^{{\small(s-1)}}
leading to the
discrete
HS black flower entropy relation

\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{N}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\sum_{s=2}^{N}\left(q_{{\small s}}+\tilde{q}_{{\small s}}\right)|\Lambda_{n_{s},p_{N}}^{{\small(s-1)}}|

(189)

For SU
2
of the spin 2 gravity model, we get
2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(q_{{\small 2}}+\tilde{q}_{{\small 2}}\right){{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{n_{2},p_{2}}
with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{n_{2},p_{2}}=n_{2}+p_{2}/2
where
n_{2}
is an integer and
p=0,1

\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits 2.
As for the higher spin SU
3
theory, we obtain
2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(q_{{\small 2}}+\tilde{q}_{{\small 2}}\right)\Lambda_{n_{2},p_{2}}^{{\small(1)}}+2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(q_{{\small 3}}+\tilde{q}_{{\small 3}}\right)\Lambda_{n_{3},p_{3}}^{{\small(2)}}
where
\Lambda_{n_{2},p_{2}}^{{\small(1)}}=n_{2}+n_{3}/2
and
\Lambda_{n_{3},p_{3}}^{{\small(2)}}=n_{3}/2+p_{3}/3

with
n_{2},n_{3}
being integers and
p=0,1,2

\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits 3.
Within this
perspective, the generators
\boldsymbol{W}_{0}^{{\small(s)}}
can be
interpreted in terms of number operators with eigenvalues N
{}_{s}^{\text{{particles}}}
(bosonic N
{}_{s}^{\text{{bose}}}
and/or
fermionic N
{}_{s}^{\text{{fermi}}}
). For instance, the ground state
with N
{}_{s}^{\text{{fermi}}}
fermions gives

\text{{S}}_{\text{{hs-bh}}}^{\text{{sl}}_{N}}=4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\sum_{s=2}^{N}N_{s}^{\text{{fermi}}}|\Lambda_{n_{s},p_{N}}^{{\small(s-1)}}|

(190)

To carry out explicit calculations, we mainly focused on the leading values
N=2,3,4
of the SL(N) family; however, the construction holds for all N.
Now, the 2D boundary potentials
a^{\text{{diag}}}=a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}+a_{t}dt
and

are generally valued in the Cartan subalgebra of
the gauge symmetry as exhibited by eqs(
28
-
33
). For the example
of the HS
SL(3,\mathbb{R})
model, we have
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}/t}=a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}/t}^{0}L_{0}+a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}/t}^{0}W_{0}
and

with L

and W

being related to the usual commuting Cartan H
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{1}}
, H
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}}
generators as in (
204
). And because
[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},a_{t}]=0
and
[\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}},\tilde{a}_{t}]=0,
the flatness
conditions of the non abelian 2D gauge curvatures reduce to

giving the dynamical
constraints
\partial_{t}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}-\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}a_{t}=0
and

.
Assuming constant Lagrange multipliers (
a_{t}=cte,

\tilde{a}_{t}=cte
),
these relations become
\partial_{t}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}=0
and
\partial_{t}\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}=0
.

In our approach, we considered the above conditions
\partial_{t}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}=0
and
