as fields equations of motion
following from a variational principle with 2D boundary field functionals
\mathcal{S}_{\text{{bnd}}}^{\pm}
interpreted in terms of total
Hamiltonian and total angular momentum. These boundary quantities are
explicitly given by eqs(
77
)-(
80
) and can be shortly presented
like

\mathcal{S}_{\text{{bnd}}}^{\pm}=\frac{\mathrm{k}}{8{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{0}^{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}dt_{{\small E}}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{0}^{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}Tr\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}\partial_{t_{{\small E}}}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\pm\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}\partial_{t_{{\small E}}}\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\right)

(191)

where the Lagrange multipliers are taken as
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}L_{0}+\Lambda^{0}W_{0}.
The boundary fields
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}\right)

are time dependent and read
explicitly like
-\frac{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}{2}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)

\times

\cos(\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}t_{{\small E}})
with
the property
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}=\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right)=-a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}=\mathrm{0}\right)=\frac{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}{2}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)
giving
vanishing velocities
\partial_{t_{{\small E}}}a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}\right)=0
and
\partial_{t_{{\small E}}}\tilde{a}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}},t_{{\small E}}\right)=0.

With this construction, we were able to successfully replicate the
previously established results of 3D BH literature and gain further insights
on the different branches of black flowers considered in
[
6
]
. Contrary
to what was shown in
[
6
]
for trivial holonomies corresponding to
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p=0}=I_{id}
, non trivial holonomies discretize the HS black holes
descent towards spin 2 black holes as there are no continuous links joining
the black flowers entropy to the spin 2’s one. In consequence, to get to the
core BTZ BH, one requires discrete transitions generated by a Weyl
transformation of the compact real from of the complexified gauge symmetry
as given by (
102
).

Additionally, we uncovered a close-knit relationship between the
entropy calculation of HS soft hair BHs and the real forms of the
complexified gauge symmetry. To study the thermodynamical properties of
black holes, we must switch to the Euclidean framework with time coordinate
t_{{\small E}}=it
which leads to a complexification of the gauge symmetry
and then to one of its real forms as described by eqs(
40
)-(
43
)
for
SL(3,\mathbb{R})

where (

black hole.

As a future perspective, we intend to develop the free field realisation to
probe the near horizon dynamics of HS black flowers as a HS extension to the
work conducted in
[
48
]
based on the Hamiltonian reduction approach. In

[
48
]
, the authors considered spin 2 gravity with near horizon boundary
conditions
a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}^{\text{{diag}}}=\mathcal{J}_{{\small(2)}}^{0}W_{0}^{{\small(2)}}
and
a_{t}^{\text{{diag}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{{\small(2)}}^{0}W_{0}^{{\small(2)}}.
And then, using the Hamiltonian
reduction method, they brought the AdS
3
gravity action to the form

S_{NH}\left[\Phi_{n},P_{n}\right]=\int dt\left(\sum_{n\geq 0}P_{n}\overset{.}{\Phi}_{n}-H_{NH}\right)

(193)

with near horizon hamiltonian
H_{NH}
given by the
J_{0}
mode of the
current
\mathcal{J}_{{\small(2)}}^{0}.
The solution to the equations of
motion of the above action obtained in
[
48
]
are similar to the primary
scalar solution (
146
) of the charge potential. In this regard, one can
gain great insights as well as substantial advantages from the free field
framework like:
\left(i\right)
more simple dynamics as given by the
action (
193
).
\left(ii\right)
Treatment of the soft hair states
using conformal field theory in accordance with the AdS
3
/CFT
2

correspondence.
\left(iii\right)
Lifting the degeneracy of the soft
hair excitations via the KdV hierarchy of modified boundary conditions.
\left(iv\right)
Using fermionisation ideas, it is interesting to study
the link between (
193
) and the free fermionic realisation we gave in
subsection 5.2. Progress in these directions in relation to HS black flowers
will be reported in a later occasion.

Appendices

We give three appendices A, B and C where we report some technical
details. In appendix A, we construct HS theories having gauge symmetries
given by real forms of complex Lie algebras. In appendix B, we derive the
asymptotic algebra obeyed by the edge invariants operators. And in appendix
C, we elaborate on the physical significance of the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
centre
symmetry giving rise to non trivial holonomies of boundary potentials of HS
black flowers.

Appendix A
HS theories with real forms of complex Lie algebras

Here, we describe the construction of real
forms of complex Lie groups (algebras) through the leading elements of the
SL(N,\mathbb{C)}
series by using graphical illustrations given by
Tits-Satake graphs. Recall that
SL(N,\mathbb{C})
has one Dynkin diagram
with
N-1
nodes. However it has N Tits-Satake diagrams corresponding to the
N real forms counting
\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
the real split
form
SL(N,\mathbb{R}),

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)
the compact real form
SU(N)
and
\left(\mathbf{3}\right)
the non compact
SU(N_{1},N_{2})
s
with
N_{1}+N_{2}=N
containing amongst others the particular subgroups

SU(N_{1})\times SU(N_{2})\subset S\left[U(N_{1})\times U(N_{2})\right]\subset SU(N_{1},N_{2})

(194)

The leading member in the
SL(N,\mathbb{C)}
family is the
SL(2,\mathbb{C)}

group which has two real forms:
\left(\mathbf{a}\right)
the real split
form

and
\left(\mathbf{b}\right)

\mathbb{C}^{2}

Case of SL(3) model

The previous construction holds also for higher dimensional Lie algebras
beyond SL(2). For instance, the complex Lie algebra
sl(3,\mathbb{C)}
has
three real forms: the real split

5

showing that

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$L_{0}$&$=$&$H_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{1}}+H_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}}$\\
$W_{0}$&$=$&$\frac{2}{3}\left(H_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{1}}-H_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}}\right)$\end{tabular}\quad,\quad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$H_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{1}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{4}\left(2L_{0}+3W_{0}\right)$\\
$H_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{4}\left(2L_{0}-3W_{0}\right)$\end{tabular}

(204)

with
\left(W_{n}\right)^{\ast}=W_{n}
and
\left(W_{n}\right)^{\dagger}=\left(-\right)^{n}W_{-n}
as well as

Tr\left(W_{0}W_{0}\right)=\frac{8}{3},\qquad Tr\left(W_{-}W_{+}\right)=-4,\qquad Tr\left(W_{-2}W_{+2}\right)=16

(205)

Accordingly, the (
L_{{\small 0}},W_{{\small 0}}
) are the generators of the
algebra
gl\left(1,\mathbb{R}\right)_{+}\oplus gl\left(1,\mathbb{R}\right)_{-}
also denoted like
gl\left(1\right)_{{\small L}_{{\small 0}}}\oplus gl\left(1\right)_{{\small W}_{{\small 0}}}.

In the next part, we develop a HS basis

using simple roots of Lie
algebra with illustrative examples. We shall first distinguish between the
usual HS basis built on
SL_{2}
representations

and the HS
basis founded on simple roots of Lie algebras (i.e
Cartan-Weyl basis). Evidently, the two HS bases are connected via bridge
transformations.

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)

HS basis using

SL_{2}

representations

The matrix representation of the higher spin generators
W_{m_{s}}^{{\small(s)}}
of the sl
N
Lie algebra relies on decomposing the total group
dimension as
\dim\left(sl_{N}\right)=\sum_{s=2}^{N}\dim\left(\boldsymbol{R}_{s}\right)
with
SL_{2}
representation dimensions given by

\dim\left(\boldsymbol{R}_{s}\right)=2s-1
with
s=j+1.
It is
constructed as follows:

(\mathbf{i})
single out the
L_{0,\pm}=W_{m_{2}}^{{\small(2)}}

expanding like
\sum_{j,k}\left|j\right\rangle(L_{0,\pm})_{jk}\left\langle k\right|
with representative matrices as

\left(L_{+}\right)_{jk}

=

-\sqrt{j\left(N-j\right)}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{j+1,k}

\left(L_{-}\right)_{jk}

=

+\sqrt{k\left(N-k\right)}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{j,k+1}

\left(L_{0}\right)_{jk}

=

+\frac{1}{2}\left(N+1-2j\right){{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{j,k}

Tr\left(L_{0}L_{0}\right)

=

+\frac{N}{12}\left(N^{2}-1\right)\equiv{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}}_{N}

(206)

then compute the powers
\left(L_{\pm}\right)^{s-1}
to get

\displaystyle\left(L_{+}\right)^{s-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\right)^{s-1}\sum_{l=1}^{N-s-1}\left(\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{n=0}^{s-2}\sqrt{\left(l+n\right)\left(N-l-n\right)}\right)\left|l\right\rangle\left\langle l+s-1\right|

(207)

\displaystyle\left(L_{-}\right)^{s-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{N-s-1}\left(\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{n=0}^{s-2}\sqrt{\left(l+n\right)\left(N-l-n\right)}\right)\left|l+s-1\right\rangle\left\langle l\right|

(208)

obeying the nilpotence property
\left(L_{\pm}\right)^{N}=0.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
consider the higher spin
W_{m_{s}}^{{\small(s)}}
as isospins
j=s-1
representations. They are accordingly
obtained by performing successive actions of
ad_{L_{-}}
on the
highest weight state
\left(L_{+}\right)^{s-1}
as follows

W_{m_{s}}^{{\small(s)}}=2\left(-\right)^{s-m_{s}-1}\frac{\left(s-m_{s}-1\right)!}{\left(2s-2\right)!}\left(ad_{L_{-}}\right)^{s-m_{s}-1}\left(L_{+}\right)^{s-1}

(209)

with the property

Tr(W_{0}^{{\small(s)}}W_{0}^{{\small(s)}})=\frac{48{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}}_{N}\left[\left(s-1\right)!\right]^{4}}{\left(2s-1\right)!\left(2s-2\right)!}\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{j=2}^{s-1}\left(N^{2}-j^{2}\right):={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}}_{N}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}}_{s}

(210)

The full structure of the HW representations is as
sketched here after

j=1

j=2

\ldots

\ \ \ \ j=N-1

L_{+}

\rightarrow_{L_{+}}

\left(L_{+}\right)^{2}

\rightarrow_{L_{+}}

\ldots

\left(L_{+}\right)^{N-1}

\ \ \downarrow ad_{L_{-}}^{m_{2}}

\ \downarrow ad_{L_{-}}^{m_{3}}

\ \downarrow ad_{L_{-}}^{m_{{\small N}}}

L_{-}

\rightarrow_{L_{-}}

\left(L_{-}\right)^{2}

\rightarrow_{L_{-}}

\ldots

\left(L_{-}\right)^{N-1}

(211)

with labels taking the values
1-s\leq m_{{\small s}}\leq s-1.

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)

HS basis using roots system

This HS basis can be derived from the above construction by using the
projectors
\boldsymbol{P}_{l}=\left|l\right\rangle\left\langle l\right|
, labelled by
l=1,...,N
, and the basic step operators
\boldsymbol{E}_{l}=\left|l\right\rangle\left\langle l+1\right|

as well as
\boldsymbol{F}_{l}=\left|l+1\right\rangle\left\langle l\right|
. These operators satisfy the properties
\boldsymbol{E}_{l}\boldsymbol{F}_{l}=\boldsymbol{P}_{l}
and
\boldsymbol{F}_{l}\boldsymbol{E}_{l}=\boldsymbol{P}_{l+1}
. As such, the previous realisation of the
sl_{2}

generators L
0,±

can be expanded in terms of (
\boldsymbol{P}_{l},\boldsymbol{E}_{l},\boldsymbol{F}_{l}
) as follows

\displaystyle L_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}\sqrt{l\left(N-l\right)}\boldsymbol{E}_{l}

\displaystyle L_{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}\sqrt{l\left(N-l\right)}\boldsymbol{F}_{l}

(212)

\displaystyle L_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\left(N+1-2l\right)\boldsymbol{P}_{l}

where the charge operator
L_{0}
can be recast as

L_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{\left[N/2\right]}\left(N+1-2l\right)\left(\boldsymbol{P}_{{\small l}}-\boldsymbol{P}_{{\small N+1-l}}\right)

(213)

For
N=2,
the
L_{0}
charge matrix reads as
\frac{1}{2}\left(\boldsymbol{P}_{1}-\boldsymbol{P}_{2}\right)
and
L_{+}=-\boldsymbol{E}_{1}
as well
as
L_{-}=+\boldsymbol{F}_{1}.

As for
N=3,
we have

L_{0}=\boldsymbol{P}_{1}-\boldsymbol{P}_{3},\quad L_{+}=-\sqrt{2}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}+\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\right),\quad L_{-}=+\sqrt{2}\left(\boldsymbol{F}_{1}+\boldsymbol{F}_{2}\right)

(214)

with the properties
L_{+}^{2}=2\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}\right)
and
L_{+}^{3}=0
. We can also take on the case with
N=4,

where

\displaystyle L_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{3}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{1}+\boldsymbol{E}_{3}\right)-\sqrt{4}\boldsymbol{E}_{2}

\displaystyle L_{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\sqrt{3}\left(\boldsymbol{F}_{1}+\boldsymbol{F}_{3}\right)+\sqrt{4}\boldsymbol{F}_{2}

(215)

\displaystyle L_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\left(\boldsymbol{P}_{1}-\boldsymbol{P}_{4}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\boldsymbol{P}_{2}-\boldsymbol{P}_{3}\right)

Moreover, using
\boldsymbol{H}_{i}=\boldsymbol{P}_{i}-\boldsymbol{P}_{i+1},

the
L_{0}
is given by a linear combination of the diagonal
\boldsymbol{H}_{i}
’s as follows

L_{0}

=

\frac{3}{2}\left(\boldsymbol{H}_{1}+\boldsymbol{H}_{2}+\boldsymbol{H}_{3}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{H}_{2}

=

\frac{3}{2}\left(\boldsymbol{H}_{1}+\boldsymbol{H}_{3}\right)+2\boldsymbol{H}_{2}

(216)

For generic
N,
we have

L_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{l=1}^{\left[N/2\right]}\left(N+1-2l\right)\left(\boldsymbol{H}_{l}+\boldsymbol{H}_{l+1}+...+\boldsymbol{H}_{N-l}\right)

(217)

In terms of the simple roots
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}
of the
sl_{N}
Lie
algebra, the above matrix realisations of the principal sl
2
can be
rewritten in terms of the generators
\left(\boldsymbol{H}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{l}},\boldsymbol{E}_{\pm{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{l}}\right)
of sl
N
as follows

\displaystyle L_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}\sqrt{l\left(N-l\right)}\boldsymbol{E}_{+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{l}}

\displaystyle L_{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{N-1}\sqrt{l\left(N-l\right)}\boldsymbol{E}_{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{l}}

(218)

\displaystyle L_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{l=1}^{\left[N/2\right]}\left(N+1-2l\right)\boldsymbol{h}_{l}

with

\boldsymbol{h}_{l}=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{n=0}^{N-2l}\boldsymbol{H}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{l+n}}

(219)

Beyond the SL(3) symmetry

The above sl(3) construction extends straightforwardly to encompass other
real forms of the
sl\left(N,\mathbb{C}\right)
family as well as other
Lie algebras of the Cartan classification. Building upon the SL(2) and SL(3)
models, we give the HS generators
W_{m_{2}}^{(2)},

W_{m_{3}}^{(3)},

W_{m_{4}}^{(4)}
of SL
4
in terms of the simple roots {

I. HS spin basis using simple roots
:

The generators
L_{0,\pm}
of the principal
sl_{2}
in terms of the
Cartan-Weyl generators
\boldsymbol{H}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{i}},\boldsymbol{E}_{\pm{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{i}}
of the Lie algebra
sl_{4}
are given by

\displaystyle L_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{3}\boldsymbol{E}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{1}}-2\boldsymbol{E}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}}-\sqrt{3}\boldsymbol{E}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{3}}

\displaystyle L_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\boldsymbol{H}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{1}}+2\boldsymbol{H}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}}+\frac{3}{2}\boldsymbol{H}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{3}}

(231)

\displaystyle L_{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\sqrt{3}\boldsymbol{E}_{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{1}}+2\boldsymbol{E}_{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}}+\sqrt{3}\boldsymbol{E}_{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{3}}

with
\left[\boldsymbol{E}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{i}},\boldsymbol{E}_{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{i}}\right]=\boldsymbol{H}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{i}}.
These expressions are invariant
under the discrete mapping
(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{3})

\leftrightarrow

(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{3},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1})
exchanging
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}

and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{3}
while preserving
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}.
This
discrete
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry is nothing other than the outer
automorphism of the Dynkin diagram of sl(4,
\mathbb{C}
). The corresponding
matrix realisation can be written as

L_{+}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\small 0}&{\small-}\sqrt{{\small 3}}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small-2}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small-}\sqrt{{\small 3}}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right),\text{ \ }L_{-}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
\sqrt{{\small 3}}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 2}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&\sqrt{{\small 3}}&{\small 0}\end{array}\right),\text{ \ }L_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\frac{3}{2}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&\frac{1}{2}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small-}\frac{1}{2}&{\small 0}\\
{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small 0}&{\small-}\frac{3}{2}\end{array}\right)

From these relations, one can deduce the highest weight states

and

+\sqrt{12}\left(\boldsymbol{E}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{1}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{E}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{3}}\right)

-6\boldsymbol{E}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{1}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{2}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}_{3}}

with

Appendix B
More on the edge invariants

In this appendix, we derive the algebra governing the asymptotic
symmetry of the edge invariants operators.

Edge invariants

(\mathtt{I}_{{\small 0}},\mathtt{\tilde{I}}_{{\small 0}})

defined as follows

\displaystyle 2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathtt{I}_{{\small n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{k{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}{4}\int_{0}^{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}e^{in{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}Tr\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\mathcal{J}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}+\Lambda\mathcal{K}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\right]

\displaystyle 2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathtt{\tilde{I}}_{{\small n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{k{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}{4}\int_{0}^{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}e^{-in{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}Tr\left[\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\mathcal{\tilde{J}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}+\tilde{\Lambda}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}\right]

\displaystyle\mathtt{I}_{{\small n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}J_{n}^{0}+\Lambda^{0}K_{n}^{0}\right]

\displaystyle\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\left[\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{0}\tilde{J}_{n}^{0}+\tilde{\Lambda}^{0}\tilde{K}_{n}^{0}\right]

At the quantum level, the above Poisson brackets get mapped to the
commutators

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left[\boldsymbol{J}_{n}^{{\small 0}},\boldsymbol{J}_{m}^{{\small 0}}\right]$&$=$&$\pm\frac{\mathrm{k}}{2}n{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{n+m,0}$\\

Appendix C
The
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
centre symmetry

In this appendix, we elaborate on the centre symmetry of the
higher spin black flowers with gauge field holonomy sitting in
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
. This finite abelian discrete group divides the gauge symmetry
\mathcal{G}
of the HS Chern-Simons model for the black flowers into N
subsets
\mathcal{G}_{p}
like

\mathcal{G}=\cup_{p=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{G}_{p}

(260)

with zero mode
\mathcal{G}_{0}\simeq SU(N)
. Because of this gradation of
the gauge group
\mathcal{G}
, the centre symmetry gives rise to holonomy
states
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{p}\right)\right\rangle

labelled by the N elements of the discrete symmetry

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}\quad;\quad 0\leq p<N

(261)

generated by
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}=e^{i2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}/N}.
In what follows, we first give useful
generalities. Then, we describe the physical states in Yang-Mills theory at
finite temperature with centre symmetry
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
.
After that,
we consider the holonomy states
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{p}\right)\right\rangle

in HS black flowers with discrete

\mathbb{Z}_{N}
.

Generalities

As a front matter of this description, we start by recalling two useful
features and turn after to give technical details.

First, recall that at classical level, a given gauge-field configuration
A

in the holonomy (
50
) and in
SU(N)
YM theory in general can be seen
as representing a certain state
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(A\right)\right\rangle
of the gauge model (HS black flowers). However, since
\mathcal{G}_{0}\simeq SU(N)
is the group of gauge transformations
U_{0}
,
the potential
A
is not uniquely defined and consequently all the gauge
configurations

A^{U_{0}}=U_{0}AU_{0}^{-1}+iU_{0}dU_{0}^{-1}

(262)

should represent the same physical state in the sense that all the
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(A^{U_{0}}\right)\right\rangle
’s describe the same
physical state as
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(A\right)\right\rangle.
Also,
this means that the physical
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(A\right)\right\rangle

is in fact in 1:1 correspondence with the
\mathcal{G}_{0}
-orbit of the
gauge configurations defined as

\mathcal{A}=\left\{A^{U_{0}}\quad|\quad U_{0}\in\mathcal{G}_{0}\right\}\qquad,\qquad\mathcal{G}_{0}/\mathcal{G}_{0}\simeq I_{id}

(263)

So, it is natural to refer to the physical state in this family of gauge
configurations just as
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{0}\right)\right\rangle
instead of
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(A\right)\right\rangle.

Second, from a general view, the above state
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{0}\right)\right\rangle
is in fact a particular state as it
is labelled by
\mathcal{G}_{0}
which is a subgroup of
\mathcal{G}.
By
considering the full gauge symmetry
\mathcal{G}=\cup_{p=0}^{N-1}\mathcal{G}_{p}
having subsets
\mathcal{G}_{p}
demanded by the non trivial actions
of the centre
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
, one then ends up with N physical states
labelled as
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{p}\right)\right\rangle
.
The relationship of these states with the centre
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
is given
by the correspondence between
\mathcal{G}_{p}/\mathcal{G}_{0}
and the
group elements
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}
(i.e:
\mathcal{G}_{p}/\mathcal{G}_{0}\leftrightarrow{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}
) indicating in turn that
\mathcal{G}_{p}

can be imagined as given by
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}\mathcal{G}_{0}.
For convenience,
we refer below to these (holonomy) states
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{p}\right)\right\rangle
simply as

\left|p\right\rangle\quad,\quad 0\leq p<N

they are individually invariant under the zero mode
\mathcal{G}_{0}
but
not under the extended
\mathcal{G}
.

To get more insight into the properties of the physical states
\left|p\right\rangle
in HS black flower, we propose to first discuss the
physical significance of the discrete symmetry
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
in Yang-Mills theories at finite temperature that is invariant
under SU(N). Then, we comment on the importance of the gauge invariant
states indexed by the group elements
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}
due to the
centre symmetry
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
. After that, we turn to our main
concern regarding the states
\left|p\right\rangle
descending from
the regularity of the holonomy (
50
) namely

W\left[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right]=\exp\left(i\int_{0}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}a_{t_{{\small E}}}^{\text{{diag}}}dt_{{\small E}}\right)

(264)

sitting in the centre
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
in the HS Chern-Simons theory for
black flowers. The algebraic setting of the states
\left|p\right\rangle
obtained in our paper can be put in correspondence with
those physical states investigated in
[
49
]
for YM at finite temperature
with which we begin this appendix.

Discrete

\mathbb{Z}_{N}

in YM theory at
finite temperature

Following
[
49
]
, the centre
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
is a discrete symmetry that
is present in SU(N) Yang-Mills theories at finite temperature. In this
theory, the gauge potential A
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
is valued in the Lie algebra of the SU(N) gauge
invariance (A
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}=\sum t_{a}
A
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}^{a}
); and it is a function of the euclidian time
\textstyle\mathchar 284
and the spatial
variables
\mathbf{x}

[
49
,
50
,
51
]
. Contrary to the
usual gauge transformations of SU(N) which are not physical, it
happens that the transformations under
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
action are physical
in the sense that they permit to jump between gauge invariant states
\left|p\right\rangle
(observables
\mathcal{O}_{p}
) of the YM theory
labelled by the group elements
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}
. This feature can be nicely
illustrated by thinking about the
\left|p\right\rangle
(observables
\mathcal{O}_{p})
in terms of the factorisation

\left|p\right\rangle=\left|0,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}\right\rangle\qquad;\qquad\mathcal{O}_{p}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}\mathcal{O}_{0}

(265)

where
\left|0\right\rangle
(
\mathcal{O}_{0}
) is a basic state
corresponding to the neutral group element
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{0}=I_{id}\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}
. An interesting example of such observables is given by the so-called
Polyakov loop
\mathfrak{l}=\left\langle\Phi\left[A\right]\right\rangle

with gauge functional
\Phi\left[A\right]
as

\Phi\left[A\right]=\frac{1}{N}tr\left[\mathcal{P}\exp\left(ig\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{0}^{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}A_{0}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)\right)\right]

(266)

where
\left[0,\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right]\equiv\mathcal{C}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}

designates the thermal cycle with temperature
T=1/\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}
and
where g is the gauge coupling constant. Below, we show that, modulo
unphysical SU(N) gauge transformations, the discrete
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
acts trivially on gauge fields A
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
but not on the functional
\Phi\left[A\right]
.
Here, we draw the main lines of the description towards (
265
) and refer
to the work of
[
49
]
for further explicit details.

The action of the discrete
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
on the potential
A
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}
and the functional
\Phi\left[A\right]
arises from
the observation that at finite temperature the usual
SU\left(N\right)

gauge potential transformations

\mathrm{A}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}^{U}=U\mathrm{A}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}U^{-1}+\frac{i}{g}U\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}U^{-1}\qquad,\qquad U=U\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)

(267)

that leave invariant the classical field action S
{}_{YM}\left[A\right]
do
not all qualify as exact symmetries under the functional integrals type (
266
). This remarkable feature is due to the periodic boundary
condition of the potential A
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)

at finite temperature which restricts the gauge field to obey the constraint

A_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}+\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}},\mathbf{x}\right)=A_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)\qquad,\qquad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\in\mathcal{C}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}

(268)

but allows for larger gauge transformations going beyond
the usual SU(N) as explicitly showed in
[
49
]
. For
concreteness, we denote these extended gauge transformations like

V\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x};{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}\right)=V_{(p)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)

(269)

with
V_{(0)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
equals to the transformation
U\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
in (
267
) that read in terms of the
group
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)=\sum t_{a}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{a}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
and the identity
I_{id}
as follows

U\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)=e^{i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)}=I_{id}+i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)+...

(270)

The
V_{(p)}
’s go beyond SU(N), they belong to an extended symmetry
\mathcal{G}
with gradation
\cup_{p=1}^{N}\mathcal{G}_{p}
. This extended
group contains N subsets
\mathcal{G}_{p}
including:
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the subset
\mathcal{G}_{0}
having a group structure isomorphic
to SU(N); it is a normal subgroup of the extended
\mathcal{G}
.
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
The other N-1 subsets
\mathcal{G}_{p}
with
p\neq 0

\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits N
are not subgroups of
\mathcal{G}
; they obey the composition
law

\mathcal{G}_{p}\mathcal{G}_{q}\simeq\mathcal{G}_{p+q}\qquad,\qquad p,q=0,...,N-1

(271)

required by the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
gradation. Actually, these commutative
compositions define the group of physical centre transformations isomorphic
to
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
.

So, given a gradation level p, the gauge transformation
V_{(p)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
sits in the subset
\mathcal{G}_{p}
; and is related to
the basic SU(N) transformation
U\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
belonging
to
\mathcal{G}_{0}
as follows

U\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}+\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}},\mathbf{x}\right)

=

V_{(p)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)

V_{(p)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)

=

e^{i\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}p}{N}}U\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)

(272)

These relations are remarkable; they indicate that the boundary condition of

U\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
holds only up to a
\mathbb{Z}_{N}

transformation
U^{(h)}=hU\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)h^{-1}
with
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}
. From eqs(
268
-
272
), one gets the following results:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)

the potential
A_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
and the gauge transformation
U\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
have the same boundary conditions only for the subgroup
\mathcal{G}_{0}\simeq SU(N)
of the extended
\mathcal{G}
. This state corresponds to
trivial holonomy in HS black flowers.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

only the changes of
\mathcal{G}_{0}

should be considered as genuine gauge transformations in the sense
of unphysical transformations which do not alter the physical states
\left|p\right\rangle
and observables
\mathcal{O}_{p}
of the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
- system. Formally, we have
\mathcal{G}_{0}\left|p\right\rangle=\left|p\right\rangle
and
\mathcal{G}_{0}.\mathcal{O}_{0}=\mathcal{O}_{0}
.

\left(\mathbf{iii}\right)

any transformation belonging to the
complement
\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{G}\backslash\mathcal{G}_{0}
should be
seen as a physical transformation that permits to jump between the states
\oplus_{p=1}^{N}\left|p\right\rangle
and observables
\mathcal{O}=\oplus_{p=1}^{N}\mathcal{O}_{p}
of the system. These transformations
allow to leap from a gauge invariant state
\left|p\right\rangle
to a
different gauge invariant state
\left|q\right\rangle
with
p\neq q.

The same for
\mathcal{O}_{p}
towards
\mathcal{O}_{q}.

\left(\mathbf{iv}\right)

for the case of the Polyakov loop, the
functional
\Phi\left[A\right]
changes as for the
YM
observables
\mathcal{O}_{p}
(
265
). So, under the gauge transformation
A\rightarrow A^{U}
, the Polyakov loop transforms like

\Phi\left[A^{U}\right]=e^{i2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}p/N}\Phi\left[A\right]

(273)

where
e^{i2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}p/N}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}
is an element of the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}

centre of SU(N). For the explicit derivation of the above relation,
we refer to the sections II and IV in the work
[
49
]
; see also eq(32)
there.

Discrete

\mathbb{Z}_{N}

in HS black flowers

The previous description done for generic YM theory at finite temperature is
also valid for HS black flowers considered in this paper. Here, the centre
symmetry
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
arises in AdS
3
higher spin theories with
SU(N) due to the Wick transformation
t=it_{E}
necessary for the study and
the computation of the thermodynamical properties like the black hole’s
entropy. For HS black flowers, we have

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}=t_{E}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{x}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}

while the role of YM potential A
{}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
is now played by the boundary potential
a_{t}\left(t_{E},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)
valued in the Cartan
subalgebra of the unitary gauge symmetry SU(N).

Given this bridging, the holonomy of the boundary potential
W\left[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right]
(
264
) can be put in correspondence with the
Polyakov loop (
266
). The properties of the transformation
W\left[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right]
under the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
centre symmetry behaves as
for
\Phi\left[A\right]
; and as such the action of the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}

is a physical symmetry. This means that even though the set of
transformations given by

a_{t}^{U}=Ua_{t}U^{-1}+iU\partial_{t}U^{-1}\qquad,\qquad U=U\left(t_{E},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)

(274)

leave the HS CS Lagrangian invariant, only some of them are considered as
symmetries of
W\left[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right]
. This is due to the periodicity
property acting differently on the SU(N) elements
U\left(t_{E},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)
in comparison to the gauge fields
a_{t}\left(t_{E},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)
. In fact, the periodicity property restricts the boundary gauge
field
a_{t}\left(t_{E},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)
as:

a_{t}\left(t_{E}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)=a_{t}\left(t_{E},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)

(275)

where
\textstyle\mathchar 268
designates the inverse of temperature. However, it allows for
a much larger symmetry for the gauge transformations because of the property

U\left(t_{E}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)=e^{2i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}p/N}U\left(t_{E},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)

(276)

and

W\left[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}^{U}\right]=e^{2i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}p/N}W\left[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right]

(277)

indicating that the
W\left[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}^{U}\right]
has a
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
gradation like
\oplus_{p=1}^{N}W_{\left(p\right)}\left[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right]
. The new states
W_{\left(p\right)}\left[a_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right]

with
p\neq 0
are demanded by the periodicity condition of boundary gauge
potential
a_{t}\left(t_{E},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}\right)
requiring in turn an extended
gauge symmetry (
260
) with
\mathcal{G}_{p}
behaving like
e^{2i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}p/N}\mathcal{G}_{0}.

At the end of this appendix and in the light of the centre symmetry
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
, one learns that a HS black flower has N physical
layers
\mathfrak{L}_{0},...,\mathfrak{L}_{N-1}
indexed
by the group elements
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{p}
. The transition
between the layers is given by discrete leaps. This property was not
apparent when considering unphysical transformations via trivial holonomies.
Additionally, we were able to establish a link between the BH entropy and
compact subgroups of the real forms of the complexified gauge symmetry and
give an algebraic interpretation to the HS BH transitions: for example, the
discontinuous passage between the HS 3 black holes and the spin 2 BH is
merely due to the fact that the centre
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is not a subgroup of
the HS symmetry centre
\mathbb{Z}_{3}.
